## Snapshot
Two-segment infrastructure-tech compounder. **Semiconductor Solutions** (~68% of rev in Q2 FY26) is now dominated by **AI semis** — custom XPUs/ASICs co-designed for hyperscalers/frontier labs plus the merchant AI-networking franchise (Tomahawk switches, Jericho fabric, 200/400G SerDes, 1.6T DSPs, CPO/optics). **Infrastructure Software** (~32%, GM ~93%) is VMware/VCF + legacy CA/Symantec, a sticky ARR annuity. The investment case is almost entirely AI-ASIC: AVGO is the de-facto primary silicon partner to **Google TPU** (12-yr relationship, 14 ASICs to date), plus Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta MTIA, and two unnamed customers (JPM believes ByteDance + Alibaba; per Harlan Sur, 2026-06-05). It also designs custom XPUs at TSMC and depends on outsourced foundry/HBM/substrate supply (FY25 10-K). Bench-marked as a structural AI-networking winner: UBS pegs AVGO at ~29% of the merchant AI-networking TAM by 2028 (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22).

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Custom AI XPUs/ASICs (e.g. Google TPU) · 2) AI/DC networking silicon (Tomahawk, Jericho, SerDes/DSPs/optics) · 3) Infrastructure software (VMware/VCF + CA/Symantec) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers / frontier labs — Google, Anthropic, OpenAI, Meta + 2 unnamed; top-five end customers a significant share of net revenue (FY25 10-K) |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC foundry (CoWoS); HBM (SK Hynix/Micron/Samsung); substrates (Singapore/SEMCO glass); back-end packaging (ASE FoCoS) |

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-14 (GS Communacopia 2026 — Broadcom broadcast session; the analyst's own notes, compiled 2026-09-14) — THE $115bn / $230bn PATH IS NOT NEW, BUT WHAT *"SUPPLY CONSTRAINED"* ACTUALLY MEANS FINALLY IS: THE CHIPS ARE LOCKED AND THE BUILDINGS ARE NOT.** ⚠️⚠️ **MANAGEMENT SOURCE (Hock Tan at a broker-hosted conference), NOT GOLDMAN RESEARCH — no rating, no price target, no estimate anywhere in the notes. The headline numbers are ALREADY ON THIS PAGE from the 09-02 call and the 09-08 Bloomberg FINAL transcript; nothing below supersedes any of them.** ([notes](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GS_Conference_2026_MY_NOTES_SUMMARY_2026-09-14.html))
- 🔴🔴 **THE NET-NEW PART — THE CONSTRAINT IS DISAGGREGATED, AND ONLY HALF OF IT IS BROADCOM'S TO SOLVE.** Broadcom has **largely locked the required 2027 wafers, memory and substrates** and is **working through 2028**; the less predictable constraint is whether customer sites have **power, buildings, permits, transformers, turbines and construction completed on time**, and **a site required in 2028 generally needs to begin construction now** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Broadcom broadcast session, 2026-09-14). ➤ **This page has carried *"supply is constrained"* since 09-02 as an undifferentiated phrase. It now splits into a COMPONENT leg that is contracted and a REAL-ESTATE leg that is not — which relocates the marginal risk from Broadcom's own supply chain to its customers' construction schedules, and makes the FY28 ~$230bn number a bet on permitting and power delivery rather than on wafer allocation.** ✅ **Same binding constraint five managements described at this conference (see the conference-level read filed on [[NVDA]]), which is why it should be scored as an INDUSTRY condition rather than as Broadcom colour.** ⚠️ **Management's framing of BOTH FY27 and FY28 as *"conservative"* is a claim, not a disclosure; and the transformer/turbine list is qualitative — no lead times, no site count and no GW figure were given.**
- 🔴 **THE SMALLER INCREMENT: THE CHIPLET LADDER GETS A COUNT.** Broadcom has moved **from one to two compute dies, is developing four-die designs, and sees a path to eight dies as one chip**, with conventional node transitions now delivering only limited performance improvement — chiplets replacing the historical Moore's Law benefit (GS Communacopia 2026 — Broadcom broadcast session, 2026-09-14). ➤ **This is the mechanical version of the advanced-packaging / high-speed-interconnect / multi-die moat argument this page already carries against [[GOOG]] insourcing and against MediaTek and [[MRVL]]. The moat LIST was on the page; the die-count LADDER is net-new, and it is the part that can actually be tracked against competitors.**
- ⚠️ **AND WHAT IS *NOT* NEW, LOGGED EXPLICITLY SO THIS SOURCE IS NOT DOUBLE-COUNTED: the ~$115bn FY27 / ~$230bn FY28 AI revenue path, the >20GW / $35bn XPV platform with Broadcom residual-value guarantees, the [[GOOG]] agreement through 2031 across every TPU generation, the six frontier-model customers with four able to self-fund, the [[OPENAI]] Jalapeño first sample in ~9-12 months plus the next two generations in development, the ~$30bn revenue / ~$10bn operating cost per GW customer economics, the ~$200bn inference-token production cost versus ~$150bn of model revenue, the record FY2026 cash into a December board decision, and the ~$56bn of low-coupon debt management does not want to repay — all already on this page. - 🔴🔴 **THE HBM-OUT-OF-BOM ARGUMENT ARRIVES *INVERTED*, AND IT IS THE BEST COUNTER THIS PAGE HAS TO THE REDBURN SHARE-CEDE MECHANISM.** Third-hand from Hock's post-earnings callback: *"what he did say to us on the callback, post-earnings call, was that their agreement with Google is **TAKE OR PAY**. It's a kind of **pre-existing sort of amounts and it INCREASES ON AN ANNUAL BASIS**."* The inference drawn from it: *"if you… think about that framework and then **HBM is taken out**, then it probably means **MORE CHIPS**, right, which is **not bad news**."* ➤ 🔴 **This page's standing bear (Rothschild/Redburn · Conor O'Mara, 06-23) is that Google shifts share to MediaTek PRECISELY BECAUSE Broadcom charges HBM inside the BOM, letting Google cut *"a gigantic part of the bill of materials."* Under a take-or-pay DOLLAR commitment that steps up annually, stripping HBM out of the BOM does not shrink the commitment — it buys MORE silicon against the same dollars. The two legs are not mutually exclusive (Google can de-BOM HBM *and* dual-source), and neither is proven; but until now the page carried only one direction.** ✅ **CONFIRMS the take-or-pay characterisation already here from UBS's 09-03 callback (*"take or pay," multi-tens of billions annually, growing*) and from Hock's own 09-08 *"TPUs and networking products through 2031"* — this is the first source to add that the contracted amounts **step up annually**.** ⚠️⚠️ **THIRD-HAND MANAGEMENT (analyst ← Hock ← private callback). **🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-08 (★ PRIMARY — THE BLOOMBERG TRANSCRIPT OF THE SAME COMMUNACOPIA SESSION IS NOW IN HAND, AND IT IS A *MANAGEMENT* SOURCE, NOT GOLDMAN RESEARCH. - 🔴🔴🔴 **⚠️⚠️ A NEW $/GW BASIS — AND IT IS *NOT* THE $20-30bn CHIP-CONTENT FIGURE THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES. DO NOT CONFLATE, NET OR CHAIN THEM.** Hock, asked where value accrues across the stack, VERBATIM: *"Take the case of a very good frontier model. **One gigawatt could generate $30 billion ARR**, as you know. **The cost to run one gigawatt, as you all know, is about $10 billion a year.** So you have **$20 billion that accrue to the model guys and the application that goes with it**, **$10 billion fought over by the power guy, the cloud provider guys, even the chip and memory guys.**"*
  - **THE BASES, LABELLED:** **(a) MODEL-LAYER REVENUE per GW ≈ $30bn of ARR** (the CUSTOMER's revenue) · **(b) COST TO RUN a GW ≈ $10bn/yr** (the CUSTOMER's opex) · **(c) VALUE TO THE MODEL/APPLICATION LAYER ≈ $20bn**, a residual of (a) − (b) · **(d) THE CONTESTED POOL ≈ $10bn**, split across power, cloud, chip and memory. **NONE of these is (e) the $20-30bn/GW HARDWARE-INFRASTRUCTURE basis or the $10-15bn/GW AVGO-CONTENT basis already on this page, none is (f) BUILD COST per GW (~$30-60bn), none is (g) the XPV FINANCING basis (<$1.75bn/GW), and none is (h) [[NVDA]]'s $18/25/40bn REVENUE per GW.**
  - ➤ 🔴🔴 **THE UNCOMFORTABLE READ, AND IT IS THE CEO'S OWN PLACEMENT OF HIS OWN COMPANY: Broadcom is not in the $20bn bucket. Hock puts *"the chip and memory guys"* inside the **$10bn that is *"fought over"*** — i.e. the largest merchant custom-silicon supplier on earth is telling a conference that his slice is the CONTESTED third of the per-GW economics while the model layer takes two-thirds uncontested. This is the arithmetic underneath the Goldman note's summary line already on this page (*"the majority of AI economics to accrue to owners of leading frontier models"*), and it is materially more specific than that summary.**
  - ⚠️🔴 **AND IT CONFLICTS WITH A NUMBER THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES, BY 2x — LOGGED, NOT AVERAGED, NOTHING DELETED.** The run-cost figure on this page is **~$5bn/GW/yr** — Hock as RELAYED by JPM's Harlan Sur on the 09-03 callback (*"~$30bn of ARR per gigawatt… against a fully-loaded cost of roughly $5bn per year"*). **From Hock's own mouth six days later the same line is ~$10bn/yr.** ⚠️ **Both marks are kept and dated. The primary is the CEO speaking directly, so it is the one to quote — but note the JPM version was [[ANTHROPIC]]-SPECIFIC while this one is generic (*"a very good frontier model"*), so they may not be the same object. Retire the ~$5bn only if the FINAL transcript confirms $10bn.** (Prior value archived in `## Changelog` with its original date.)
- 🔴🔴 **★ XPV, DEFENDED IN THE FIRST PERSON — THE CEO'S OWN ANSWER TO THE CIRCULAR-FINANCING CHARGE, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED ONLY AS PRESS (FT 08-04), CREDIT-DESK COMMENTARY AND A THIRD-PARTY RECONSTRUCTION (Epoch 08-12) UNTIL NOW.** Verbatim: *"We do it by creating a platform, **we call it XPV**, where we will enable them to be financed. **We do not finance directly.**… So I'll be direct, **this is not circular financing.** When a customer sees the demand that drives up their own demand and they don't have to use us necessarily, they can get it somebody else, that's not circular finance. **We're using financing to create demand.**… we **harness a bunch of financial partners and banks who are willing to come in and essentially take the risk on financing [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]], at least partially.** And on the balance of it… **we will provide backstop in the form of residual guarantees on what is the equipment that is secured against that.**"* On why the banks show up at all: *"they believe in the demand, but two is **rates**. They get better rates trading through this."*
  - ➤ ✅ **THIS CORROBORATES THE STRUCTURE THIS PAGE ALREADY MODELS — residual-value support, second-loss against the RACKS rather than first-loss against the lease — but as MANAGEMENT ON THE RECORD rather than as journalism. Two things it does NOT do: it does not size anything (the $29bn maximum-exposure cap and the $30bn-of-$35bn backstop coverage stand exactly where they are), and it does not answer the credit desks' actual question, which is what *"the equipment that is secured against that"* recovers in a default. See `## Risks`.**

Re-check against the FINAL transcript.** Hock neither restated nor corrected the parameters.
- 🔴 **THE SIX-CUSTOMER ROSTER GETS A QUALIFIER IT DID NOT HAVE: ALL SIX ARE FRONTIER-MODEL DEVELOPERS.** *"we have **only six customers today** at this point, very concentrated group, but I believe these six customers, **all each of them are creating their own frontier models, LLMs**… **Of the six, two of them, two of the STRONGEST guys, do not have the cash flows** to be able to generate the compute needed to train and create inference, productize their models."* ➤ **The page already carried *"six customers total, four described as going to be huge"* and *"we are doing financial vehicles for 2 customers, the other 4 can fund themselves"* from the 09-02 call, so the roster and the funding split are NOT new. - 🔴 **[[OPENAI]] / JALAPEÑO — THE FORWARD PIPELINE IS NOW TWO GENERATIONS DEEP, WHICH IS NET-NEW.** *"needless to say, relationship is very strong and continues to be very strong because **we are working on the NEXT TWO GENERATIONS to the jalapeno**."* On the first one: *"within a year, we created an XPU called a jalapeno, which **performs as well, if not better, than the best state-of-the-art general-purpose accelerator out there**"* — the CEO's own version of the merchant-GPU parity claim, against the **10GW custom-silicon supply agreement signed last October** and the first sample delivered **within nine months** (announced end-June). ➤ **The page carried *"the next OpenAI XPU is approaching tape-out"* (09-02 call) as a single dateable milestone; management now describes a TWO-generation forward roadmap. Prior scope is not superseded — it is extended.** The mechanism he credits is the same one that produced the nine months: *"When you have that very tight, almost seamless flow, it's not that much a challenge to be able to create chips of that caliber that quickly."*
- 🔴🔴 **★ THE OPEN-WEIGHT VS CLOSED-MODEL ECONOMICS, WITH NUMBERS — THE MOST QUOTABLE THING HOCK SAID, AND THE FIRST TIME THIS WIKI HAS THE WHOLE SLIDE FROM A CEO'S MOUTH.** Underlying data attributed by Hock to **OpenRouter and Vercel**; all figures INFERENCE-ONLY (*"Trading [training] is not easily monitored, so it's not just inference"*). **Total cost of generating the world's tokens ≈ $200bn/yr against ≈ $150bn of model-layer revenue earned on them.** The split: **half or MORE of the TOKENS come from open-weight players, but *"75% at least"* of the REVENUE comes from frontier closed models** ⇒ **frontier ≈ $120bn of revenue on ~$100bn of spend; open-weight ≈ $30bn of revenue on ~$100bn of spend.** Hock: *"You think that's a sustainable model? We don't know, but… **the value goes to where intelligence continues to improve**"*, and on the five-year view the value chain *"will reside with **the leading edge frontier models**, as opposed to the open weight models, even though they may coexist."*
  - ➤ ⚠️ **HANDLE WITH THE BASIS ATTACHED: this is a MODEL-LAYER P&L, not a semiconductor one, and the $200bn is a COST OF GENERATING TOKENS (inference infrastructure) — it is not hyperscaler capex, not the $/GW ladder, and it must not be netted against either. Its use on this page is directional: it is the CEO's stated evidence for why he underwrites six frontier-lab customers and no open-weight ones, and it is the same argument as the $20bn/$10bn split above. Both are Hock's characterisation of third-party data — not audited, not a Broadcom disclosure, and all of it narrative text rather than a chart.**

- **INTERNAL AI ADOPTION — AND THE ROI CASE THE CEO ACTUALLY ENDORSES IS NOT HEADCOUNT.** **15,000 of Broadcom's 30,000 engineers engaged** with frontier and open-weight models, *"in earnest now for one year"*, across hardware and software design. The quantified use case: *"if they have a bug and have to re-spin, **that's SIX MONTHS AND $30 MILLION for a leading edge chip**"* — and the tools *"could help us eliminate the need for that re-spin."* On productivity: *"in terms of just simply **productivity improvements, the return on investment is DEBATABLE**. (AVGO): Communacopia + Technology 2026 — Key Takeaways", presenter **Hock Tan, President & CEO**, **2026-09-08**)
- 🔴 **"Broadcom REAFFIRMED its FY27/FY28 AI revenue targets of $115 BN / $230 BN, while emphasizing that SUPPLY IS CONSTRAINED."** The constraint language is the incremental part — the targets themselves were already on this page.
- 🔴🔴 **"Broadcom expects the MAJORITY OF AI ECONOMICS TO ACCRUE TO OWNERS OF LEADING FRONTIER MODELS."** ➤ **This is the CEO of the largest merchant custom-silicon supplier telling a conference that his customers, not he, capture most of the value. Read against Tan's own 09-06 margin framing on this page — "stop focusing on gross margin… look at OPERATING MARGIN" because AI memory content structurally dilutes headline GM — the two statements are the same argument seen from two ends: Broadcom is taking a large revenue share of a pie whose economics it does not expect to own. That is a directional negative for the terminal-margin case and it came from management unprompted.**
- 🔴 **"The $35 BN XPV PARTNERSHIP is designed to accelerate AI infrastructure buildouts, FUNDING MORE THAN 20GW OF COMPUTE CAPACITY while MINIMIZING BALANCE SHEET RISK."** ⚠️ **$35bn funding >20GW implies <$1.75bn of XPV structure per GW — which is NOT a build cost and must not be compared to the ~$30-60bn/GW build-cost anchors or to [[NVDA]]'s $18/25/40bn revenue/GW. It is the financing layer, not the capex.** See [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- 🔭 **CORROBORATION FROM THE CORPUS ON THE ANTHROPIC LEG, NOT ADOPTED: Cantor (via @cazenove_uk, 09-08) says "Broadcom points to 10GW OF [[ANTHROPIC]] CAPACITY IN 2028, alongside major [[OPENAI]], [[GOOG]] & [[META]] requirements", and that "2028 could require 40 TO 50GW of AI capacity, with leases need signing now." Separately @TheTranscript_ re-circulated Tan's F3Q26 call line that "for OpenAI, JALAPENO is on track for the planned deployment of 1.3 GIGAWATTS IN 2027."** ⚠️ **The 10GW Anthropic figure is a relay of a relay and is not in the GS note — flagged, not adopted; but it is the first attempt to size the Anthropic commitment in GW and it is ~5x the OpenAI Jalapeno 2027 number.**
- **SUPPLY-CHAIN CROSS-CHECKS FROM THE SAME WINDOW: MS's Howard Kao says Broadcom's planned Singapore substrate capacity carries LIMITED RISK to Unimicron/NYPCB because "AVGO appears to be securing incremental FC-BGA supply through AST, ITS JV WITH TOPPAN, rather than vertically integrating substrate manufacturing", with PHASE 1 OF THE TWO-PHASE SINGAPORE FAB TARGETED TO BEGIN PRODUCTION BY YEAR-END 2026. And [[SAMSUNG]] is reportedly among Broadcom's suppliers-in-waiting on two fronts: Taylor 2nm bookings (with [[TSLA]] and [[ARM]]) and silicon-photonics foundry evaluation. Also from CRN: "AVGO VMware price hike fears are 'settling down' in the AI era for clients and partners."**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-06 — HOCK TAN PUTS A GIGAWATT NUMBER AND A GENERATION COUNT ON THE META MTIA PROGRAM, VERBATIM.** (@TheTranscript_ quoting the AVGO F3Q26 earnings call, **2026-09-06**)
- 🔴 ***"Our partnership with Meta to deliver multiple generations of MTIA XPUs remains on track. Between now and the end of 2027, we will be delivering 3 GENERATIONS of MTIA accelerators to Meta. Across these 3 generations, we have LINE OF SIGHT TO DEPLOY 3 GIGAWATTS THROUGH '28."***
- ➤ **Three things this pins down that the page previously carried only as framework: (1) the CADENCE — three MTIA generations inside roughly five quarters, faster than any merchant GPU cycle; (2) the UNIT OF ACCOUNT — Hock is again quoting GIGAWATTS, not chips or dollars, consistent with the $/GW ladder already on this page; (3) the HORIZON — "through '28", i.e. the [[META]] relationship is underwritten past the end of the FY28 framework.** ⚠️ **"Line of sight" is not backlog. And do NOT convert 3 GW into revenue using the blended ~$13bn/GW figure on this page — MTIA is one customer and one architecture, not the blended book.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — HOT CHIPS PUTS BROADCOM INSIDE FIVE DIFFERENT ACCELERATOR PROGRAMS AT ONCE, AND THE JALAPEÑO DESIGN CYCLE IS THE HEADLINE NUMBER.** (J.P. Morgan · **Harlan Sur**, Hot Chips 2026 takeaway, relayed **2026-09-07**)
- 🔴 **The Jalapeño ASIC was "built with AVGO in ~9 MONTHS via AI-accelerated co-design"** — and, notably, **its design metrics were presented in terms of PARETO FRONTIER POSITIONING (latency, energy per request, tokens/kW) rather than peak FLOPS.** ➤ *The metric change is the story: when buyers score on tokens/kW rather than FLOPS, the co-design house captures more of the value than the IP owner does.*
- ➕ **09-08 — THE CUSTOMER'S CFO ADDS THE MECHANISM BEHIND THE NINE MONTHS: OpenAI's Jalapeño *"taped out in nine months, with the model running optimizations engineers did not have time to get to in the final 30 days before the design went to TSMC"*** (Sarah Friar, OpenAI CFO, GS Communacopia fireside, 2026-09-08 — relayed notes, relay author unconfirmed; full block on [[OPENAI]]). ➤ **First-party version of the "model-designed chip" line (Vital Knowledge, 09-03). She also listed "the in-house chip" as one leg of a compute stack that is "a spectrum, not an either-or" alongside CSP partners and self-build — the customer, not Broadcom, framing custom silicon as one option among several.**
- 🔴 **"AVGO stands out as the most underappreciated cross-cutting beneficiary, appearing across Google TPU, Jalapeño, Meta MTIA, SambaNova RDU, and its own Thor Ultra 800G Ethernet NIC, reinforcing its dual role in both AI compute silicon AND AI networking."**
- **Custom ASICs and merchant GPUs are complementary, not substitutive; custom XPU share is expected to trend toward PARITY with merchant GPU over several years.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — HARLAN'S POST-PRINT NUMBERS, AND THE BULL/BEAR THE DESK ACTUALLY HEARD.** (J.P. Morgan — **Harlan Sur** (research: **OW, Dec-27 PT $580** ≈ 25x CY27E EPS of **$23.60**) + **Joshua Meyers** (spec sales, *not research*), 2026-09-07)
- **The print, restated for the record:** revenue **$29.6bn (+86% y/y)** ≈ in line with Street; **AI semiconductor revenue $16.7bn (+54% q/q, +221% y/y)** the standout; **XPUs ~73% of AI revenues** with **XPU shipments up >3.5x y/y**; **F4Q guide $34.8bn (+93% y/y)** in line, implying **FY26 AI revenue ~$58bn vs the prior ~$56bn framework.**
- **The framework: AI semi revenue doubling to ~$115bn in FY27 and doubling again to ~$230bn in FY28, with ~$30 EPS guided for F28.** Harlan calls these conservative: *"if supply constraints mirror NVDA's (~80-85% of order book), 'unconstrained' demand could be $145bn+ in FY27 and $270bn+ in FY28."*
- 🔴 **Customer count made explicit: FOUR >10% AI customers expected in FY28 — [[GOOG]], [[META]] and the two leading frontier model makers** — and *"their top frontier model client set to become their largest XPU customer in FY27 with ~5 GW of TPU v8i deployments."*
- ⚠️⚠️ **THAT LAST CLAUSE IS INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT AS RELAYED, AND IS NOT ADOPTED: "TPU v8i" is a Google part, not a frontier-lab part. Either the ~5 GW belongs to GOOGL and the sentence conflates two customers, or a frontier lab is taking v8i capacity THROUGH Google. Do not carry "~5 GW to a frontier lab" as a fact pending the primary note.**
- **On the TPU-competition worry: "AVGO is already shipping v8i AHEAD of MediaTek's v8t."** ⚠️ See [[MEDIATEK]] — JPM's own Asia analyst takes the opposite side of this inside the same weekly, and the desk prints "we'll have to agree to disagree."
- 🔭 **Desk colour (Bernstein spec sales · Tyler Seidman, 2026-09-07 — *not research*): "AVGO conversations skewed more NEUTRAL as the week progressed (vs knee-jerk negative post-print) on their exposure to Anth/OAI, though without a clear catalyst path in 2H the stock feels STUCK into year-end as it becomes a SHOW-ME STORY in '27/'28."** Jefferies' desk is blunter: **"AVGO continues to feel like the FUNDING SHORT amongst Semi specialists"** (Jefferies Tech · J. Favuzza, 2026-09-07). AVGO closed the week **-3%**.

**🆕 2026-09-07 — SUPPLY-CHAIN READ-THROUGH: THE BROADCOM/TOPPAN SUBSTRATE JV IN SINGAPORE IS ALREADY INSIDE THE ABF SUPPLY/DEMAND MODEL, NOT INCREMENTAL TO IT.** (BofA · **Mike Yang**, "NYPCB: Addressing concerns on BT price outlook & ABF substrate supply", **2026-09-07**, Buy, PO NT$1,700 vs price NT$1,055)
- Investors marked Nan Ya PCB down **12-13% in two sessions (vs Taiex +~1%)** partly on that Singapore capacity. BofA's answer: **"Broadcom/Toppan's JV in Singapore is NOT a new/additional capacity for substrate in our view, given that it takes at least 1-2 YEARS to have the relevant facility ready ahead of the MP in FY27"** — and it is already inside BofA's August-2026 ABF S/D model. ➤ **For AVGO the fact that matters is the DIRECTION, not the size: Broadcom is putting its name on upstream SUBSTRATE capacity with a mass-production date of FY27, at exactly the moment ABF is named as one of the two hard AI bottlenecks (MS, Shawn Kim, 09-07). File under vertical integration, not under supply relief.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-04 — THE $/GW CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED, BY THE SAME ANALYST WHO CREATED IT: HOCK'S $20-30bn IS SILICON *PLUS* SYSTEMS-LEVEL HARDWARE; BROADCOM'S OWN SILICON IS ~$13bn/GW. AND THE ASP DISCLOSURE THAT KILLS THE PRICE-EROSION BEAR CASE ARRIVED WITH IT.**

_(Source: **JPM · Harlan Sur, post-F3Q26 "Ask Me Anything" follow-up call, 2026-09-04 — Capstone-attended recording, PRIMARY.** ⚠️ **ASR transcript: "Hawk" = Hock Tan; "COT" = the in-house Google silicon team this wiki has logged as "CLT"; "Tranium" = Trainium; "Samannova" = SambaNova; "Halopenyo" = Jalapeño. Names normalised here; the garbling is recorded so nobody re-derives a new entity from it.)_

- ✅🔴🔴 **THE RESOLUTION, VERBATIM AND UNAMBIGUOUS.** *"The **$20 billion to $30 billion per gigawatt** figure mentioned by Broadcom's CEO… includes Broadcom's silicon content **PLUS all systems-level hardware infrastructure (boards, rack-scale architecture, power supply, cooling)** that sits inside the data center and uses Broadcom chips. **This figure does not include land, power, or shell costs.**"* ➤ **So the 09-02 primary transcript ("our content" in terms) and the 09-03 callbacks ($10-15bn) were BOTH right about different things: Hock was describing the hardware Broadcom's silicon sits inside, not Broadcom's revenue. The item logged 09-03 as *"a management correction or four independent mis-hearings"* is CLOSED — it was neither; it was a definitional gap.**
- 🔴 **AND THE SILICON LADDER IS NOW SPECIFIC BY CUSTOMER, WHICH IS BETTER THAN THE BLENDED NUMBER: **~$13bn/GW average for Broadcom's AI silicon (compute + networking), INCLUDING HBM** · **$11-12bn/GW for [[GOOG]] TPU — LOWER because Google's proprietary optical circuit switch architecture displaces Broadcom networking content** · **$13-15bn/GW for other XPU programmes ([[OPENAI]] Jalapeño, [[META]] MTIA, SoftBank/ARM, ByteDance, Alibaba, Baidu, SambaNova) which *"make heavy use of Broadcom's networking solutions."*** ➤ ✅ **The OCS explanation is net-new and it is the mechanism the wiki was missing: Google is a lower-content customer BECAUSE of a specific architectural choice, not because of pricing pressure.** ✅ **It also validates the 09-03 arithmetic ($115bn ÷ 10GW = $230bn ÷ 20GW = $11.5bn/GW) and therefore the withdrawal of the 09-02 "conservatism bridge" stands.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE ASP DISCLOSURE — THE STRONGEST ANSWER YET TO THE PRICE-EROSION BEAR CASE.** **TPU: V7 "Ironwood" ~$13,000/chip → V8i (ramping now) ~$15,000 → V9 *"quite a big step up."* Networking: switching ASPs are DOUBLING EVERY GENERATION — Tomahawk 3→4, 4→5, 5→6.** ➤ 🔴 **AND THE RECONCILIATION WITH HOCK'S "FLAT DOLLARS PER GIGAWATT", WHICH IS THE WHOLE [[NVDA]] DISAGREEMENT: per-chip ASPs rise, but **power per XPU rises too, so fewer units fit inside a fixed gigawatt envelope** — the two roughly cancel. So AVGO's content per GW is flat-to-slightly-up while its content per CHIP compounds. **That is a different claim from "ASPs are eroding" and it should retire that bear point.** The narrow live dispute with NVDA (content per GW rising mid-teens → mid-20s → ~$40bn) is unaffected and stays open on `themes/custom-asic-tpu.md`.**
- 🔴 **[[GOOG]] — THE "DISCONCERTING" 09-03 ITEM GETS A BENIGN EXPLANATION, THOUGH NOT A PROOF.** Harlan's account: **Google has the largest installed base of AI compute on earth — eight TPU generations over 12 years, *"tons and tons of Ironwood"*** — so it can *"be more efficient with its compute spending"* and optimise existing assets rather than buy greenfield. **Broadcom's growth at Google is put at 10-15% in 2027.** **The division of labour: Google's internal team does TRAINING chips (*"less complex"*); Broadcom does the INFERENCING ASICs (*"more complex"*).** ⚠️⚠️ **NOTE THE DRIFT AGAINST THE 09-03 ENTRY: the page carried JPM at *"+10-12% FY27 then +30-35% FY28"*; this call says **10-15% in 2027** and does not repeat the FY28 acceleration. The prior figure is moved to `## Changelog`. And note the inversion versus how this wiki previously described the split — earlier notes had the in-house team taking training as an ENCROACHMENT; Harlan frames it as Broadcom keeping the harder, higher-content half.**
- ✅ **HARLAN DISMISSES THE IN-HOUSE-COMPETES-FOR-INFERENCE BEAR CASE ON A RESOURCE-ALLOCATION ARGUMENT: it would mean *"Google having TWO INTERNAL SILICON DESIGN TEAMS WORKING ON IDENTICAL PROGRAMS,"* an inefficient use of *"precious resources"* — and Broadcom has already secured [[ANTHROPIC]]'s TPU inferencing needs through **V9i in 2028**.** ⚠️ **This is an inference from org logic, not a disclosure. It is a reasonable one, and it is the page's first argued rebuttal rather than a flat contradiction.**
- 🔴 **[[ANTHROPIC]] — SIZED, AND WITH A FINANCING BACKSTOP NAMED. **15GW of Broadcom capacity across 2027-28 (5GW in '27, 10GW in '28)**, underwritten by the **Blackstone/Apollo XPV programme.** Broadcom's underwriting case, relayed: **$65bn ARR on only 2GW with $10bn of COGS**, *"reports Anthropic was EBITDA profitable in the June quarter,"* and cost-per-token falling **60-100%+ per year** across AVGO/NVDA/AMD.** ⚠️⚠️ **This is the vendor characterising its customer's P&L. 22V's independent bottom-up on the SAME 2GW produces ~$45bn — ~30% lower. Both are logged on [[ANTHROPIC]]; neither is adopted here.** 🔴 **The genuinely new competitive fact inside it: *"[[NVDA]] is the NUMBER THREE compute supplier to Anthropic"* — behind [[AMZN]] Trainium (training) and [[GOOG]] TPU (inference).**
- 🔴 **[[META]] — THE PROGRAMME COUNT IS BIGGER THAN THE PAGE CARRIED: **FIVE distinct MTIA XPU ASICs with Broadcom through 2028**, spanning recommendation, inferencing, and *"full-blown inferencing plus training engines."*** (The page previously carried *"3 MTIA generations through '27 with visibility into 3GW through 2028"*, which is not contradicted — it is a subset. Both marks kept, dated.)
- **VMware — nothing new, and Harlan says so.** Security exploits *"have not been a significant focus area for investors"*; Nutanix competition below the top-1,000 accounts *"has been part of the VMware thesis since the acquisition"* by design, as 3-year contracts roll off.
- 🔴 **HARLAN'S TRADE FRAMING, WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT IS FALSIFIABLE: Broadcom's current underperformance and Google-related *"noise"* mirrors [[MRVL]]'s Trainium/Microsoft episode last year; he expects AVGO to outperform as *"estimate beats, COT [in-house] team underperformance, and frontier model IPOs"* dispel it.** ⚠️ **Note the third leg is an [[ANTHROPIC]]/[[OPENAI]] IPO catalyst — this house is explicitly trading AVGO as a proxy for the labs' listings.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-03/04 — THREE MORE HOUSES ON THE SAME PRINT, PLUS THE PRESS NUMBERS THE PAGE DID NOT HAVE.**

- **Rothschild & Co Redburn — BUY, *"All-In on the Frontier."*** *"The **$115bn AI revenue guidance was A TOUCH BELOW EXPECTATIONS**. But the **$230bn AI revenue guide for FY28 marks a clear strategic pivot, with THREE-QUARTERS OF THE REVENUE EXPECTED TO COME FROM [[ANTHROPIC]] AND [[OPENAI]]**. This closely binds Broadcom's equity story to **the commercial evolution of the frontier labs**, as well as **diluting the risk from further erosion in the share of Google TPU-derived revenues**."* **Valuation: EPS >$30 in FY28 implies **12.2x P/E — a ~30% DISCOUNT to the S&P 500**.** (R&Co US Riser, 2026-09-04.) ➤ **The bear and bull are the same sentence: concentration in two private, pre-IPO counterparties.**
- **Jefferies · Blayne Curtis — sees UPSIDE to $230bn.** *"October guidance was only modestly ahead… the key takeaway was a stronger than expected long-term AI outlook, with management raising its **F27 AI revenue target to $115B (vs $100B prior)**… **We believe there is room for this to push higher AS THE $/GW MOVES HIGHER.** Anthropic and OpenAI are expected to be the largest AI customers and account for the majority of the **20GW deployment pipeline** discussed for F28, while **AI networking is also expected to grow AS FAST AS XPUs**."* ⚠️ **Named gating items: *"power availability and data center readiness could influence deployment timing, while GROSS MARGINS remain a topic of debate as XPU mix and HBM content continue to rise."*** (2026-09-03.)
- **THE REPORTED FINANCIALS, FROM THE PRESS, FOR THE RECORD: revenue **$29.6bn, +86% y/y** in the quarter ending Aug 2; **AI chip revenue +221% y/y to $16.7bn**; FY26 AI revenue expected **~$57bn**; and Broadcom *"projected that growth in AI chip revenue would accelerate **236%** in the quarter through October."* FY25 EPS was *"just below $5"* against the **$30+ FY28 framework.** (The Information · Martin Peers, 2026-09-03.)
- 🔴 **PRODUCT/ARCHITECTURE — A CPO POSITIONING STATEMENT THAT CUTS AGAINST THE CONSENSUS FRAMING: *"Broadcom argued that **co-packaged optics offers a stronger fit for SCALE-OUT connectivity than SCALE-UP networks**, highlighting the growing importance of optical integration as AI clusters expand."*** (Jefferies Asia TMT · Conor O'Mara, 2026-09-04.) ➤ **Reflect into `themes/optical-cpo.md`: the CPO opportunity is being placed at the scale-OUT layer by the largest merchant switch vendor, which is where [[CIEN]]/[[LITE]]/[[COHR]] coherent content also lives.**
- **AND THE CEO'S OWN FRAMING OF WHO HIS CUSTOMERS BECOME, first-party via the corpus: *"Long term, we see these guys [[[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]]] to be **no different from a hyperscaler** and will run their own data centers and be **first party to offer AI, generative AI, APIs, SaaS models to the world**"*** (@TheTranscript_, corpus, 2026-09-03).

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-03 (★ PRIMARY — THE JPM CALLBACK *TRANSCRIPT* IS NOW IN HAND AND SUPERSEDES THE INTERNAL RELAY OF THE SAME CALL): "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback", Harlan Sur (JPM semis analyst), moderated by Josh (JPM), held the day AFTER the 2026-09-02 print and AFTER JPM's own management callback with Hock Tan — so it carries management clarifications, not only broker opinion. THE RELAY GOT THE NUMBERS RIGHT. WHAT THE PRIMARY ADDS IS A FAIR VALUE ABOVE JPM'S OWN PT, A MANAGEMENT CLARIFICATION THAT RE-CUTS THE $/GW BASIS, AN ASR GARBLE THAT HAS BEEN PROPAGATING ON THIS PAGE AS "CLT", AND ONE INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY INSIDE JPM'S OWN CALL THAT IS ITSELF THE DATAPOINT.** ⚠️ **ASR transcript — decode before quoting: "Hawk" = Hock Tan · "CLT" / "customer on tooling" = COT (customer-owned tooling) · "Halapeno" = Jalapeño · "IMIB" = EMIB · "CoS" = CoWoS.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_09_03_jpm_avgo_call.html)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **⚠️⚠️ $/GW UNIT GUARD — FOUR SEPARATE BASES ARE SPOKEN IN THIS ONE CALL. THEY ARE NOT COMPARABLE, NOT NETTABLE AND NOT CHAINABLE. QUOTE THE BASIS WITH EVERY NUMBER.** (JPM "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-03)

| Basis | Figure | What it actually measures | Whose P&L |
|---|--:|---|---|
| **Total hardware-INFRASTRUCTURE investment /GW** | **$20-30bn** | The number **Hock threw out on the 09-02 call**; on the JPM callback he **CLARIFIED it is NOT Broadcom content** — *"that's not a Broadcom content per gigawatt, that is more of a… hardware infrastructure, **which obviously includes the chip**"* | The BUYER's capex |
| **AVGO DOLLAR CONTENT /GW** | **$11-15bn** | Broadcom's own silicon + networking content, **customer-dependent**: **$11-12bn** for [[GOOG]] internal + [[ANTHROPIC]] (*"Google has its own specialized cluster design, which uses less Broadcom networking and more of Google's own proprietary networking"*); **$13-15bn** for [[META]] / [[OPENAI]] / SoftBank Arm / ByteDance / Alibaba (*"those customers do take advantage of quite a bit of Broadcom networking"*). **JPM uses ~$13bn as the mix-adjusted average** | **AVGO REVENUE** |
| **[[ANTHROPIC]] ARR /GW** | **~$30bn** | The **CUSTOMER's own annual revenue** on the DC capacity it already has | The LAB's revenue |
| **Fully-loaded COST /GW/yr** | **~$5bn** | The **CUSTOMER's** fully-loaded annual cost on that same gigawatt | The LAB's cost |

  ➤ **JPM's read, and it is the bull argument in one line: the market back-solved ~$11-12bn/GW out of the 10GW math and treated it as the ceiling; Sur says that *"just reflects the conservatism because in actuality… mix adjusted for their customer mix, it really should be around 13 billion"*, which is why he calls $115bn/$230bn *"extremely conservative."*** ⚠️ **AND THE 10GW MATH COVERS ONLY THE TOP FOUR CUSTOMERS** — *"he didn't talk about ByteDance, Alibaba"* — so ByteDance, Alibaba, SoftBank Arm and SambaNova sit OUTSIDE it (below). ⚠️⚠️ **THIS NARROWS BUT DOES NOT CLOSE THE 09-03 CONTRADICTION: JPM renders the $20-30bn as *hardware infrastructure INCLUDING the chip*, whereas BofA renders it as *"an overall cost"*, UBS as *"total system cost, encompassing power, cooling and other components"* and MS as *"the total cost of building an entire data center."* Those are NOT the same denominator. Both renderings are dated 2026-09-03; the page carries both and collapses neither.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE GUIDE-vs-ORDER-BOOK ARITHMETIC IS MECHANICAL, AND IT IS THE SAME 80-85% SHIP-RATE JPM APPLIES TO [[NVDA]] AND [[MRVL]].** FY27 order book **$140-150bn**; supply constraints allow shipment of *"80 to 85% of total orders in their backlog"* ⇒ **$115-125bn implied. JPM previewed $130bn** (*"we actually thought that they could do a bit better than that given that they are bringing on Samsung as a second source"*); **AVGO guided $115bn — the LOW END of its own implied band — and Sur reads that as deliberate: *"I actually think that this was used to kind of set up… for them to say that they're going to double their revenues in 28 to 230 billion."*** **FY28 $230bn ≈ 85% of an order book JPM sizes at $270-280bn**, versus **sell-side consensus $180bn and buyside ~$205-210bn.** ➤ **Read-across, same pattern, three vendors: JPM says [[NVDA]]'s book *"supports 15% to 20% more shipments next year than what they guided to."*** (JPM "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-03)
- 🔴 **FY26 SCOREBOARD, PLUS THE ONE-YEAR MEMORY CHECK THAT IS THE POINT OF THE WHOLE CALL.** July results + the October guide imply **FY26 AI revenue ~$58bn vs the prior $56bn guide, +186% y/y** — against a Street that, *"if we rewind back a year ago… most of us were discounting 70, 80%"* growth. Scoring the print itself: **both the July AI result AND the October AI guide beat SELL-SIDE consensus; July also beat BUYSIDE, but the October guide *"came in a little bit short of buyside."*** ➤ **Sur's use of it: the FY26 track record is the reason to discount *"the relatively conservative guides that they put out for next year and for 28."*** (JPM "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-03)
- 🔴🔴 **FY28 EPS LADDER — AND A FAIR VALUE ABOVE JPM'S OWN STANDING PT. THESIS DRIFT, LOGGED.** AVGO put out **~$30 of FY28 earnings power**; **sell-side $25**; **buyside $28-29**; **JPM is *"in print today"* at $32-33 of earnings power, which *"still implies 600 fair value"*** (*"just put a [couple] multiple on it and easily get to 600 in fair value"*). ⚠️ **This page's standing JPM mark is Overweight, PT $580 (Sur, 09-01 preview, 23x $25.35 exit-FY27 earnings power; also in `## Debate / thesis` → "Where the sell-side stands"). The $600 is stated as FAIR VALUE on a client callback and the transcript does NOT say the target was moved — carry $580 as the published PT and $600 as JPM's fair value until a note confirms otherwise. Prior mark archived in `## Changelog`.** (JPM "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur, 2026-09-03)

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-03 — FOUR HOUSES RAN POST-PRINT CALLBACKS ON THE SAME F3Q26 EVENT AND THE RESULT IS ONE RESOLVED AMBIGUITY, ONE NEW STRUCTURAL DISCLOSURE, AND ONE UNBRIDGED CONTRADICTION ON GOOGLE.** Sources: **MS · Joe Moore callback**, **BofA · Vivek Arya callback**, **UBS · Nathalia Winkler callback**, **JPM callback (relayed by Daniel Grozdea)** — all 2026-09-03, all readouts of Broadcom management post-F3Q26.

  - **WHAT THE PRIMARY SAYS (Bloomberg transcript, F3Q26 call, 2026-09-02, already on this page):** Hock, asked directly — *"the dollars per gigawatt will remain in the $20-30bn level of **OUR CONTENT**"* and *"what we are seeing per gigawatt is in the range of LESS THAN $30 billion — $20 to $30 billion per gigawatt."* **He attributed the $20-30bn to Broadcom's own content, in terms, on the call.**
  - **WHAT FOUR HOUSES SAY MANAGEMENT MEANT, ONE DAY LATER:** that the $20-30bn is the **TOTAL DATA-CENTER BUILD COST**, and Broadcom's own content is **$10-15bn/GW**.
  - ➤ 🔴 **THIS IS NOT A RELAY-vs-PRIMARY PROBLEM — the callbacks are a SEPARATE, LATER event (post-print management callbacks, 2026-09-03), so the ordinary "primary wins" rule does not settle it. It is a management CORRECTION, or four houses independently mis-hearing the same clarification.** **The wiki adopts $10-15bn/GW (~$13bn blended) as AVGO CONTENT because it is corroborated four ways, is internally consistent with the analyst's own $11-12bn GW arithmetic, and reconciles the guide; the primary's "our content" wording is PRESERVED above and in `## Changelog` as what was actually said on 09-02.** ⚠️ **If a subsequent AVGO disclosure re-asserts $20-30bn as content, this page has the contradiction dated on both sides.**
  - **BofA · Vivek Arya:** *"Broadcom stated that **its content, as a component of the overall datacenter build, is in the $10-11 billion range per gigawatt, compared to an overall cost of $20-30 billion per gigawatt.**"*
  - **UBS · Winkler:** *"The $20-30 billion per gigawatt figure cited previously refers to the **total system cost**, encompassing power, cooling, and other components, **not Broadcom's content alone.** Broadcom's content per gigawatt is lower, implied to be in the **$10-15 billion** range."*
  - **MS · Joe Moore:** *"revenue capture for AI ASICs has consistently been described as **'low teens billion per gigawatt'**. This figure **specifically includes networking**… There was some confusion around a $20 to $30 billion number mentioned, which was clarified as **the total cost of building an entire data center** around the ASIC architecture, not Broadcom's revenue capture."*
  - **JPM — the most granular, and it is a MIX, not a single number:** **Google/[[ANTHROPIC]] TPU content $11-12bn/GW** (*"slightly lower due to Google's specialized cluster design, which utilizes less Broadcom networking"*); **[[META]], [[OPENAI]], Softbank Arm, ByteDance, Alibaba $13-15bn/GW** (*"these customers leverage more of Broadcom's networking"*); **mix-adjusted average ~$13bn.**
  - ➤ **THE RULE FOR THIS WIKI: AVGO content per GW is $10-15bn depending on customer networking attach (~$13bn blended). The $20-30bn/GW is a BUILD-COST number and belongs in the same column as the ~$30-60bn/GW build-cost anchor — never in the same column as [[NVDA]]'s $25-40bn/GW REVENUE per GW, and never chained.** ⚠️ **This is the fourth distinct $/GW basis to circulate on this wiki; the side-by-side table lives on `themes/custom-asic-tpu.md`.**
- 🔴🔴 **NEW STRUCTURAL DISCLOSURE — BY FY27-28 BROADCOM'S TWO LARGEST AI CUSTOMERS ARE FRONTIER LABS, NOT HYPERSCALERS, AND GOOGLE FALLS TO THIRD.** JPM publishes the actual split: **FY27 — [[ANTHROPIC]] 49% of AI revenues, Google internal 29%, [[OPENAI]] 13%, [[META]] 10%** (*"four customers will exceed 10% of AI revenues"*); **FY28 — Anthropic 48%, OpenAI 24%, Google internal 19%, Meta 10%.** MS independently: *"Google proper is projected to be Broadcom's **3rd largest customer by 2028**… the top two customers will be **'frontier AI labs'**."* UBS independently: frontier labs *"could potentially surpass Google in XPU spend **by next year**."* ➤ **Three houses, same direction. The single largest bear point on this name — Google concentration — is being replaced by a DIFFERENT concentration: two privately-held, pre-IPO labs at ~73% of FY28 AI revenue on JPM's numbers.**
  - **Beyond the headline four, JPM adds customers absent from the GW math: ByteDance + Alibaba ~$6bn combined next year, and Softbank Arm $3bn in purchase orders for next year.**
- 🔴🔴 **UNBRIDGED CONTRADICTION — LOGGED, NOT AVERAGED: DOES GOOGLE'S BROADCOM BUSINESS GROW OR SHRINK?** Three houses relay management saying it grows; **BofA's own allocation arithmetic says Google's gigawatts DECLINE, and management deflected the question.**
  - **GROWS:** JPM — *"Google's internal TPU business with Broadcom is still growing (estimated **10-12% YoY in FY27, then 30-35% in FY28**)"*, protected by a **5-year TPU revenue agreement covering V8/V9/V10/V11**. UBS — LTA is **"take or pay," multi-tens of billions annually, growing.** MS — *"Broadcom still has a contract with Google for 'tens of billions of revenue' that 'will continue to grow.'"*
  - **SHRINKS:** BofA · Vivek Arya — *"Broadcom's total allocated gigawatts are expected to go from roughly **5 GW this year (where Google accounts for 4-4.5 GW) to 10 GW next year and 20 GW in 2028.** However, the analyst's calculations, based on specific customer allocations provided by Broadcom, indicate that **the gigawatts allocated to Google will mathematically decline (to ~3 GW next year and even less in 2028).** When pressed on this, management's response was, **'You should ask Google why they are not investing as much,'** which the analyst found **disconcerting**."*
  - ➤ 🔴 **THE RECONCILIATION IS PROBABLY REVENUE-vs-GIGAWATTS AND IT IS NOT INNOCENT: dollar revenue can grow on a take-or-pay LTA and on higher-content later TPU generations while the physical GW allocated to Google falls. But BofA's number is checkable and management's answer was a deflection, not a rebuttal. ⚠️ DO NOT NET THESE. The falsifier: Google's own capex/TPU disclosures, and whether AVGO ever restates Google GW rather than Google dollars.**
- 🔴 **THE GOOGLE ROADMAP HAS BIFURCATED — AND BROADCOM KEPT THE HARDER HALF.** JPM: *"the **'CLT' team is handling training chips (less complex), while Broadcom will continue designing the more complex inferencing chips.** FY27 is a transition year."* Broadcom's share of **total** TPU units (Google internal + external incl. Anthropic): **~66% in FY27, ~75-80% in FY28**; total TPU business growth for AVGO **+115% in FY27, +70% in FY28.** ➤ **The "Google share loss" headline and the "Broadcom TPU volume doubling" headline are both true and describe different denominators.**
- ✅ **TPU V9 2nm CANCELLATION RUMOURS EXPLICITLY REFUTED.** JPM: *"Rumors from Asia about Broadcom's next-generation **TPU V9 2nm program being canceled or delayed were explicitly refuted.** Management confirmed TPU V9 2nm is **on track to ramp for calendar 2028**,"* supporting Anthropic's 2028 inferencing requirements and Google internal inferencing.
- 🔴 **SUPPLY — BROADCOM SHIPS ONLY 80-85% OF ITS ORDER BOOK, AND THE ORDER BOOK IS FAR ABOVE THE GUIDE.** JPM: **FY27 AI revenue guide $115bn against an order book of $140-150bn; FY28 guide $230bn against $270-280bn.** *"The discrepancy between guidance and order book is attributed to supply constraints, akin to [[NVDA]]'s and [[MRVL]]'s situations, where **only 80-85% of orders can be shipped.**"* **Mitigation: [[SAMSUNG]] being brought on as a 2nm/3nm SECOND-SOURCE FOUNDRY in 2H next year.** ⚠️ **Prior FY26 AI guide $56bn; JPM implies ~$58bn, +186% y/y.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS ERROR CAUGHT INSIDE ONE OF THE READOUTS — DO NOT PROPAGATE IT.** **MS · Joe Moore's note describes *"a formalized target of $115 billion for the OVERALL OPPORTUNITY… previously $120B."*** **That is wrong on this page's evidence: $115bn is the FY27 AI REVENUE GUIDE, and $230bn is FY28** — per JPM's callback and, independently, per Jefferies' own headline the previous evening (*"Broadcom says **$115bn in 2027 $230bn 2028** demand exceeds supply"*, JEF Asia morning TMT, 2026-09-02). ➤ **A guide-year figure was re-labelled as a lifetime TAM inside a single house's summary. The wiki adopts the FY27/FY28 revenue-guide reading (two independent sources) and flags the MS framing as a mislabel.**
- 🔴 **CONTENT-PER-GW GROWTH IS NOW AN EXPLICIT AVGO-vs-NVDA HEAD-TO-HEAD, WITH A STATED MECHANISM ON BOTH SIDES.** BofA: *"Broadcom's management (Hock) articulated a view that **content per gigawatt does not grow across product generations.** Their reasoning: **as power consumption increases, fewer units can be deployed in a gigawatt datacenter, and even if ASPs double, the halving of units balances it out.** This directly contrasts with [[NVDA]]'s stated view, where content per gigawatt has increased **from mid-teens to mid-20s (Hopper/Grace Blackwell) and is expected to reach close to $40 billion for Vera Rubin**, driven by a broader ecosystem of switches, CPUs, LPUs, and SSDs."* And Hock addressed it directly: *"'Yes, I heard what my competitor are saying about rising content per gigawatt.' But he said that **the content doesn't grow per gigawatt.**"* ➤ **A clean, dated, falsifiable disagreement between the two largest AI-silicon vendors about the SHAPE of their own growth: AVGO = units only; NVDA = units × content. Filed to `themes/custom-asic-tpu.md`.**
- **COMPETITION — MEDIATEK SIZED, MARVELL DISMISSED (AND THE DISMISSAL IS NOT UNANIMOUS).** **[[MEDIATEK]]:** MS — direct TPU replacement, **has delivered functional silicon**, targeting **~20% of Google's ASIC SAM**, *"consistent with Broadcom's 80% capture assumption"*; headwinds are **HBM4 sourcing cost (AVGO procures HBM4 below market) and yields**; MS's judgement is that *"a few billion dollars per gigawatt of cost savings on a $30B data center"* does not justify replacing a proven incumbent. UBS — Broadcom holds *"the more complex and higher-volume chip design"* at Google. **[[MRVL]]:** UBS relays Broadcom calling Marvell's Google program *"a **science project**"* and *"a CPU attach,"* not the mainstream TPU roadmap, adding that **the Google team members who initiated it have since departed for [[ANTHROPIC]]**. ⚠️ **MS explicitly declines to accept that framing: *"Hock Tan's dismissal of it as a 'science project' is noted as COMPETITIVE SNIPING,"*** and reads Marvell as building complementary disaggregated low-latency solutions with a sizeable opportunity **beyond a two-year horizon**. ➤ **Two houses, same quote, opposite weight. Logged both ways; [[MRVL]]'s mid-October Analyst Day is the adjudication date.**
- **OTHER MARKS FROM THE CALLBACKS.** **Software stabilises at ~$8.7bn in Q4** — UBS: management concedes the earlier *"exponential growth"* was *"a step up and then normalization,"* **not a durable revenue growth driver**, and *"the street's expectations might have been a bit aggressive."* **Networking: ~90% share in 1.6T DSP with [[NVDA]] itself a customer**; ToR *"very profitable"*; **but management says these *"do not move the needle quite as much as some XPU products."*** **CPO: Bailey's deployed; Tomahawk 6 in early test systems, not production; AVGO leading an OCI standard consortium with [[MSFT]] and [[META]], Tomahawk 6 OCI support expected within ~6 months.** **Singapore substrate fab comes online in September**, targeting ~1/3 of leading-edge chip requirements, and over 18-24 months gains **full advanced packaging supporting up to 15x logic compute die per package** — JPM positions this as *"potentially ahead of [[TSM]] and [[INTC]]'s roadmaps."* **AVGO will support any memory format (HBM, SRAM, DRAM), not just HBM.**
- ⚠️ **A CUSTOMER-ECONOMICS CLAIM THAT IS THIRD-HAND AND SHOULD BE TREATED AS SUCH: JPM relays management saying [[ANTHROPIC]] generates ~$30bn of ARR per gigawatt against a fully-loaded cost of ~$5bn per GW per year**, and that Anthropic *"was already operating profitably at the operating income level in the June quarter."* ➤ 🔴 **A 6x gross spread on a private company, asserted by its SUPPLIER, with no disclosure behind it. Logged because it is the stated basis for Broadcom's confidence in frontier-lab funding — NOT adopted as an [[ANTHROPIC]] financial. BofA's readout frames the same monetisation differently: *"$30 billion per gigawatt, with the silicon layer keeping only $10 billion."***
- ⚠️ **NAME GARBLE IN A SOURCE, FLAGGED SO IT IS NOT PROPAGATED: the MS callback summary twice renders Anthropic as *"Anthropic (Anduril)"*.** Anduril is an unrelated defence company. **Treated as a summariser artefact; every Anthropic datapoint above is filed to [[ANTHROPIC]], none to Anduril.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-02 — F3Q26 PRINT: IN LINE ON THE QUARTER, LIGHT ON THE FQ4 GUIDE, A MISS ON FY27 AI AND A ~28% BEAT ON THE FIRST-EVER FY28 AI GUIDE. THE STOCK FELL ~3-4% AFTER HOURS ON THE MOST CONSTRUCTIVE CALL AVGO HAS GIVEN.**

- **THE QUARTER (all non-GAAP unless stated):** **Revenue $29.6bn, +86% y/y** — a record, as were operating profit and FCF · **Gross margin 75.0%** · **Operating margin 67.9%** (non-GAAP operating income **+92% y/y**) · **EPS $3.32** · **CF from ops $14.2bn · FCF $13.67bn**. By segment: **Semiconductor Solutions $20.8bn** · **Infrastructure Software $8.8bn, +29% y/y, ARR +15%** (VMware private-AI-cloud announced) · **non-AI semis (broadband, server storage, less wireless) +5%**.   - **Ironwood (TPU v7) was delivered in high volume to [[ANTHROPIC]] AND [[GOOG]] AT THE SAME TIME** — management's own framing, and the simultaneity is the substance: one part ramping into two end customers concurrently is why the Anthropic ramp does not mechanically read as Google share loss.
