## Risks
- 🔴 **🆕 2026-09-08 — TERMINAL VALUE-CAPTURE RISK, VOLUNTEERED BY THE CEO AND QUANTIFIED.** On Hock Tan's own per-GW arithmetic, **~$30bn of ARR generated per GW less ~$10bn/yr to run it leaves ~$20bn accruing to the model/application layer, against ~$10bn *"fought over by the power guy, the cloud provider guys, even the CHIP AND MEMORY guys"*** — Broadcom included, by his own naming. ➤ **The risk is not to the FY27/FY28 revenue framework, which he REAFFIRMED in the same session; it is to terminal margin and to the multiple. It compounds two exposures already in this section — customer concentration in a handful of frontier labs, and the structural GM dilution from rising memory content — and being the CEO's own framing makes it hard to dismiss as a bear talking-point.** ⚠️ **UNIT GUARD: this ~$30bn/GW is MODEL-LAYER REVENUE and the ~$10bn/GW/yr is COST TO RUN. Neither is AVGO chip content ($10-15bn/GW) nor the $20-30bn hardware-infrastructure basis. Never net or chain them.** ⚠️ **The ~$10bn/yr run-cost also sits at 2x the ~$5bn/GW/yr this page carries from JPM's 09-03 relay of the same CEO — both kept and dated; see `## Current state` and `## Changelog`.** (Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference — **MANAGEMENT: Hock Tan, President & CEO** · BBG **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴 **🆕 2026-09-08 — THE BACKSTOP, RESTATED BY MANAGEMENT, LEAVES THE RESIDUAL-VALUE QUESTION EXACTLY WHERE THE CREDIT DESKS PUT IT.** Hock: Broadcom *"do not finance directly"* and instead *"harness a bunch of financial partners and banks who are willing to come in and essentially take the risk on financing [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]], at least partially"*, with AVGO providing *"backstop in the form of **residual guarantees on what is the equipment that is secured against that**"* — alongside the flat denial that this is circular financing (*"we're using financing to create demand"*). ➤ **First-person confirmation of the structure, and it names the EQUIPMENT as the collateral — precisely the input MS Credit and Bernstein flagged as unmodellable (what a used TPU/XPU rack recovers in a default). Management gave no recovery assumption, no size, and no update to the $29bn maximum-exposure figure, so every exposure item above stands unchanged.** (Goldman Sachs Communacopia + Technology Conference — **MANAGEMENT: Hock Tan, President & CEO** · BBG **INITIAL DRAFT** transcript, 2026-09-08)
- 🔴 **CONTINGENT, OFF-BALANCE-SHEET CREDIT EXPOSURE TIED TO THE ASIC FRANCHISE — now corroborated by an independent reconstruction, not just modelled.** AVGO provides a credit backstop on **$30bn of the $34.5bn of debt financing more than 1 GW of Google TPU systems leased to [[ANTHROPIC]]**, with a **reported maximum exposure of $29bn**; the senior **A1 ($6bn, T+1pp) and A2 ($24bn, 5.75%)** tranches are backstopped, the junior **B ($4.5bn, 8.5%)** is not. On an Anthropic default AVGO **can take over the five-year lease or arrange a rack sale, then covers remaining A1/A2 shortfalls** — i.e. **second-loss against used-TPU-rack residual value, which is the unmodelled input.** Exposure is **drawn in ~16 stages, ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027**, so it **rises into mid-2027 before amortising** — today's drawn balance is not the peak. AVGO does **not** fund the $30bn in cash; it lends its credit ("vendor-supported financing"). (Epoch AI · Campbell Hutcheson, 2026-08-12; reconciles with JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16 and AVGO's 10-Q.) ⚠️ **The 2.75pp A2-vs-B spread is an UPPER BOUND on what the backstop is worth to lenders (it also embeds seniority) — not a price for the guarantee.**
  - **🆕🔴 THE FUNDING CHANNEL BEHIND THIS KIND OF STRUCTURE WENT FROM ZERO TO $35BN IN ONE YEAR, AND IT IS THE STANDOUT LINE IN MORGAN STANLEY'S CREDIT TABLE (MS US Credit Strategy · Vishwas Patkar / Fernanda Lima / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Carolyn Campbell / Eva Baurmeister / Christina Sigler, "A Break from the Heat, For Now", 2026-08-14).** Issuance by channel, YTD prior-year → YTD current (% change): **US HY 15 → 41 (+176%) · US leveraged loans 2 → 10 (+394%) · non-USD IG hyperscalers 15 → 62 (+313%) · EUR HY ([[CRWV]]) 0 → 2 · TOTAL 189 → 382 (+102%) · "CHIP FINANCING" VIA 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS 0 → 35.** ➜ **A brand-new, $35bn-and-counting private-placement channel dedicated to chip financing is the market infrastructure that makes vendor-supported structures like the $30bn Anthropic/TPU backstop repeatable rather than exceptional. That is two-sided for this page: it is how the ASIC franchise gets financed at scale (constructive for volume), and it is how much contingent, off-public-market exposure the complex is accumulating (the risk this bullet exists to track).** **MS's own framing points the same way — financing should shift *"increasingly toward chips and components, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support,"* with balance-sheet and market capacity ample but increasingly price-sensitive, *"making wider spreads the natural release valve"*; MS favours COLLATERAL-BACKED risk.** ⚠️ **The spread context is the reassuring half, and it should be quoted with the number: YTD spread change (bp) by bucket — HQ Hyperscalers +29 · Hyperscalers incl. [[ORCL]] and [[SPCX]] +26 · **[[NVDA]]+BROADCOM +20** · Semis +2 · Tech +11 · IG +1. The NVDA/AVGO credit complex widened 20bp YTD, i.e. LESS than the hyperscalers — the credit market is differentiating inside tech and is charging the chip vendors less than the buyers of their chips.** ⚠️ **None of this is an AVGO-specific spread, rating or estimate: MS's credit desk publishes no AVGO mark, and the 4(a)(2) figure is an industry channel total, not Broadcom issuance.**
  - **🗓️🔴 AND ONE DATED WATCH FLAG FROM THE FOLLOW-UP TRACKER: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"* (MS Global Credit Strategy & Securitized Products · Lima / Campbell / Tirupattur / Patkar / Egan / Becker / Loke / Verma / Tyler / Konetzke, "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider", 2026-08-20).** ➜ **The FQ3 print on 2026-09-02 is now a credit-desk event as well as an equity one, and it lands into a market MS expects to keep widening (*"a busy fall and continued spread widening"*; ~$445bn issued across credit channels YTD versus $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025, FY26E $500–550bn; Semiconductors specifically at **$42bn YTD, +57%**, having run 5, 0, 0, 8, 0, 25, 0, 5 Jan→Aug).** ⚠️ **Why it matters for THIS bullet rather than as generic macro: Broadcom's contingent exposure is drawn in ~16 stages with ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027, so the cost and availability of the private/structured channel between now and then is an input to the franchise, not a backdrop.**
  - **🆕🔴🔴 THE SPECIALIST CREDIT DESK NOW SIZES THE EXPOSURE AT ~$290–480bn, AND IT CLOSED ITS BONDS ON IT (Morgan Stanley TMT **Credit** Research · Lindsay A Tyler, with Nishant Satyam — "AVGO Credit: More Compute, More Contingency?", 2026-06-19, and "AVGO Credit: TPUs, Take Two?", 2026-08-05).** ⚠️ **CREDIT research: no equity rating, no equity PT, and MS discloses it is a paid advisor to Broadcom on the AI XPV Platform. Nothing here supersedes an equity mark on this page.** The arithmetic behind the number this bullet has been carrying only qualitatively: **the $35bn deal / $29bn max RVS supports 1.2–1.5GW ⇒ $19.3–24.2bn of maximum RVS exposure PER GW ⇒ ~$290–480bn of theoretical maximum exposure if replicated across the >20GW AI XPV platform** (MS headline ~$350bn on 06-19; by 08-05, with a second tranche reported and ~17GW still implied, *"AVGO's theoretical maximum supported exposure could surpass ~$300bn"*, while the ~$480bn top end *"now appears punitive following APCHIP amortization disclosures"*). **Treated as debt, that scenario puts gross adjusted leverage at 3.3x by FY28E versus Moody's 2.5x gross downgrade threshold** (net adjusted stays at 1.8x versus S&P's 3x) — so in MS's own framing *"the issue therefore would more be a hypothetical capital call, rather than the ratings impact."* 🔴 **The structural point to hold onto is not the leverage table but the COLLATERAL: the tail risk is *"heightened by the lack of an established TPU/XPU secondary market. TPUs appear less fungible and liquid than GPUs, with third-party sales only recently disclosed by [[GOOG]]."*** ➜ **AVGO's backstop is second-loss against the residual value of used TPU racks in a market that does not yet exist — which is why the same desk that recommended AVGO bonds on 05-29 ("a potential entry point") had closed every one of those recommendations by 06-19 and, by 08-05, called the level "not … a compelling re-entry point", with AVGO 10Y in line with the BBB index and Aa1/AA [[NVDA]] only ~20bp tighter.** ⚠️ **Two-sided, and the offsetting half must travel with it: the same desk sizes the OPPORTUNITY — AVGO's AI XPV alone at 20GW could drive *"an additional ~$430-550bn of TPU/XPU financing beyond the first tranche"* — and the mitigants are real (S&P leverage near 1x against a 3x threshold; >$5bn of planned debt reduction; execution constraints on power, labour and supply chain slowing the path to 20GW; and risk-sharing with Apollo and Blackstone Credit plus possible [[GOOG]] shell-lease backstops given its ratings capacity).**
  - 🆕 **AN INDEPENDENT THIRD PARTY NOW DESCRIBES THE SAME STRUCTURE — AND ADDS THE LEG THIS PAGE NEVER HAD: A *SECOND* GUARANTOR, [[GOOG]], STANDING BEHIND THE DATACENTRE RENT WHILE BROADCOM STANDS BEHIND THE EQUIPMENT (SemiAnalysis (Daniel Nishball, Oliver Kennon, Terence Ong) "Nvidia's Backstop Universe – Heads I Win, Tails Who Loses?", 2026-09-11).** ⚠️ **READ-THROUGH, NOT PRIMARY COVERAGE. The note is about [[NVDA]]; AVGO appears only because SemiAnalysis says it *"drew inspiration from the Google-Broadcom-Anthropic-Apollo TPU structure to surmise how [NVDA's] deals could be structured."* It does not claim to hold the documents — this is a characterisation of a private structure, not disclosure.** Mechanics as stated: *"if [[ANTHROPIC]] is unable to continue to service lease payments to the TPU SPV, then the structure can be resolved by Broadcom assuming the lease or by the racks being sold. If the racks are sold, then the residual value guarantee takes effect, as Broadcom must then make the senior noteholders whole after such equipment sale – effectively plugging the residual value gap, if any."* ✅ **That tracks this bullet's own *"can take over the five-year lease or arrange a rack sale, then covers remaining A1/A2 shortfalls"* almost word for word — third-party corroboration, no new number.** 🆕 **NET-NEW, AND THE REASON TO CARRY IT: *"Google will play its part by also backstopping rental payments to the various datacenter operators. The effect is that lenders to the TPU SPV are effectively taking Broadcom credit risk while lenders to the datacenters are taking Google credit risk."* → the page has only ever held Broadcom's leg. The equipment SPV and the datacentre shell are guaranteed by two different balance sheets, and AVGO's is the one written against the DEPRECIATING asset — precisely the used-rack residual value this bullet already calls the unmodelled input.** 🔴 **IT ALSO SHARPENS THE 08-24 COVERAGE COMPARISON WITHOUT REPLACING IT: SemiAnalysis independently confirms [[NVDA]]'s cap at *"only up to 25%"* of a deal and argues NVDA's structure *"would appear to wean investors away from this dual backstop"* because it *"is silent on any datacenter backstop"* — i.e. the TPU structure is the MORE generous of the two vendor backstops and Broadcom carries the heavier residual-value leg.** ⚠️ **BASIS GUARD: do NOT net this against The Information's 82%. That 82% is coverage of DEAL ONE's debt stack; SemiAnalysis's claim is about the NUMBER OF GUARANTORS (two vs one). Two separate facts pointing the same way — neither supersedes the other.**
- **Customer concentration:** top-five end customers a significant share of net revenue (FY25 10-K); AI rev increasingly tied to a handful of frontier labs — Sur flags AVGO is *more* exposed to OpenAI/Anthropic frontier-lab risk than NVDA (2026-06-17).
- **Supply chain:** dependence on a limited number of contract manufacturers/foundries (TSMC), HBM and substrate (FY25 10-K); back-end packaging cited as the FY27 supply constraint (UBS Arcuri, 2026-06-04). AVGO's stickiness as a quasi-foundry to hyperscalers cuts both ways — exposes it to price pressure once CoWoS capacity eases (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20). Citi's "DRAM Supply Is the New Ranking Metric" (2026-06-29) assumes TPUs ~85% memory-fulfilled — memory allocation an emerging gate even for the #2-ranked mega-cap.
- **Margin mix:** structural GM dilution as low-margin ASICs scale faster than software/networking; HBM pass-through (~$2.5k/TPU) a quantified drag (Fubon/Jefferies).
- **Share loss / COT:** Google CLT-team (MediaTek) optionality; TPU8 routed to Alchip (StoneX/John Su, 2025-08-20); longer-term COT-model bypass risk (Jukan, briefing 2026-05-18).
- **Macro/AI-capex cyclicality & power availability** gating multi-GW deployments; geopolitical/China (InP, export controls).

## Intra-quarter — calls, commentary & reports (since the F3Q26 print, 2026-09-02)

🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (/run-inbox 23h) — ★ THE BERNSTEIN PRIMARY IS NOW ON DISK, AND IT CARRIES THREE THINGS THE 21h SPEC-SALES RELAY DID NOT. **(1) THE ANTHROPIC 5GW IS EXPLICITLY *ANTHROPIC-SPECIFIC* DEMAND, SEPARATE FROM GOOGLE'S INTERNAL TPU DEMAND — "THEY ARE WRITING THE CHECKS", REGARDLESS OF WHERE IT IS HOSTED. (2) MANAGEMENT SAYS THE $115bn/$230bn OUTLOOK IS *BELOW* THE CUSTOMER GW ROADMAPS — "IF CUSTOMERS ULTIMATELY DEPLOY THE FULL GW AMOUNTS, REVENUE WOULD BE HIGHER." (3) BROADCOM CLAIMS SITE-LEVEL VISIBILITY: 2027 SITES "LARGELY COMPLETE", 2028 SITES "SUFFICIENTLY ADVANCED."**

(Bernstein · **Stacy A. Rasgon, Ph.D.**, *"U.S. Semiconductors and Semicap Equipment: Notes from the road — takeaways from management meetings in Silicon Valley"*, **2026-09-21**; the Broadcom session was with **Hock Tan (President & CEO), Amie Thuener (CFO) and Ji Yoo (Head of IR)**. **AVGO rated OP, TP $575.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_260438.html). ⚠️⚠️ **MANAGEMENT-SOURCED — a broker-hosted private meeting, paraphrased by the analyst. NOT company disclosure, NOT Reg-FD'd.** ⚠️ **This is the PRIMARY behind the Tyler Seidman spec-sales relay in the block immediately below; the Sunfish-continuity and supply-led-gating lines there are confirmed and are not repeated.**)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE ANTHROPIC DOUBLE-COUNT WORRY IS ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE MOST VALUABLE LINE IN THE NOTE FOR THIS PAGE.** *"the demand attributed to Anthropic is separate from Google's internal TPU demand. Specifically, Broadcom characterized the 5GW Anthropic opportunity as Anthropic-specific demand (they are writing the checks), regardless of where the infrastructure is ultimately hosted."* ➤➤ **THIS CLOSES THE PRODUCT-LINE FORK THE 09-21 NOTION-INGEST OPENED ON THIS PAGE. JPM's Harlan Sur had [[ANTHROPIC]] on [[GOOG]] TPU through CY28 with its own ASIC only at architectural-spec stage, which raised the question of whether the Anthropic 5GW and the Google TPU line were the same silicon counted twice. Management says they are not: the 5GW is Anthropic's cheque, wherever the racks physically sit. ⚠️ Note it does NOT say the silicon is a different part — it says the DEMAND and the PAYER are different, which is a commercial answer to a technical question.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **MANAGEMENT SAYS ITS OWN GUIDE SITS BELOW THE CUSTOMER ROADMAPS, IN ITS OWN WORDS.** *"its AI revenue outlook and customer GW roadmaps are related but not directly equivalent: the GW figures represent customer demand, while revenue forecasts incorporate judgment around how fully that demand can actually be deployed. If customers ultimately deploy the full GW amounts, revenue would be higher than the current outlook of $115B/$230B in 2027/28, respectively."* ➤➤ **THIS IS MANAGEMENT ITSELF CONFIRMING THE MECHANISM BEHIND THE "SANDBAGGING" ARITHMETIC THE PAGE LOGGED FROM TECH FUND ON 09-19 AS AN AUTHOR'S DERIVATION — and it is consistent with JPM's 09-03 callback arithmetic (order book $140-150bn × 80-85% ship-rate = $115-125bn implied, AVGO picked the BOTTOM). THREE INDEPENDENT ROUTES NOW POINT AT THE SAME CONCLUSION: the guide is a deployment-haircut on the roadmap, not a demand ceiling. ⚠️ NO SIZE IS GIVEN FOR THE HAIRCUT — "higher" is the only quantification management offered. The $115bn / $230bn framework is UNCHANGED and is NOT superseded.**
- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE SITE-LEVEL VISIBILITY CLAIM, WHICH IS THE DIRECT ANSWER TO THE 2028-VARIABILITY BEAR ON THIS WIKI.** *"Broadcom has detailed visibility into the physical sites underlying its 2027-28 forecasts, noting that 2027 sites are largely complete, and 2028 sites are already sufficiently advanced to provide confidence in the outlook."* ➤➤ **SET DIRECTLY AGAINST Jefferies' Blayne Curtis on the 09-18 bus-tour recap call (logged on [[NVDA]]): *"27 is pretty solid… But when you look at 28, there's a lot of VARIABILITY, and that's what people have been fearful of."* Curtis and Broadcom management agree completely on 2027 and disagree on 2028 — and Broadcom is claiming site-by-site knowledge rather than a forecast. ⚠️ Unverifiable from outside; it is an assertion, and it is the assertion on which the $230bn rests.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE DIVERSIFICATION CLAIM, AND IT REFRAMES THE WHOLE GOOGLE-SHARE DEBATE.** *"management believes OpenAI and Anthropic could each become at least if not larger than Google, making the custom accelerator business considerably more diversified by 2028"*, and the explicit rebuke to the sell-side's focus: *"investors are too focused on Google share risk relative to the size of the incremental opportunities at OpenAI, Anthropic and Meta. Google already has a large installed compute base, while the frontier labs are building capacity from much lower starting points, creating significantly greater incremental silicon requirements."* ➤ **The bear case on this page has been a SHARE question about one customer; management's answer is a DENOMINATOR argument. Both can be true.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE NEAR-TERM PULL-INS, WITH META'S TIMING NARROWED.** Management expects **sequential growth through 2027**, with **[[OPENAI]] looking to pull in its roadmap and accelerate Jalapeño deployment BEYOND what is embedded in Broadcom's current outlook**, and **[[META]] similarly attempting to pull in — although Meta remains primarily a 2028 opportunity with POTENTIAL INITIAL REVENUE IN LATE 2027.** ➤ **"Late 2027" is a tighter Meta date than this page has carried; the BofA/TrendForce MTIA roadmap row (09-16) can now be checked against it.** ⚠️ **Gating factors named as physical infrastructure and semiconductor supply rather than demand — specifically power, wafers and memory.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE DISMISSALS ARE BLUNTER IN THE PRIMARY THAN IN ANY RELAY.** On the TPU alternative: *"MediaTek participates in pieces of the TPU opportunity but does not provide the same end-to-end architecture."* On the nearest listed peer: management *"characterized [[MRVL]]'s current exposure as XPU-adjacent rather than a meaningful custom accelerator business."* And on the technical trajectory: challenges are *"becoming substantially harder with every generation, particularly around high-speed mixed-signal design and SerDes."* ⚠️ **A vendor's characterisation of a competitor — logged as management's view, NOT adopted as a fact about [[MRVL]]'s business.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE CONTENT-PER-DEPLOYMENT ASYMMETRY, WHICH IS NEW TO THIS PAGE AND IS THE MARGIN-RELEVANT ITEM.** *"With less mature silicon organizations such as OpenAI and Anthropic, Broadcom is helping define the XPU, memory subsystem, networking architecture and rack design, which should create MORE CONTENT PER DEPLOYMENT than with an established customer such as Google."* ➤➤ **THIS MATTERS FOR THE `$/GW` ROW IN THE SIGNAL TABLE. Hock's $20-30bn/GW is an average across a customer base whose MIX is shifting toward the higher-content lab customers. It is an argument for the top of that range as OpenAI and Anthropic scale — and it is the first management statement on this page that the per-GW content is customer-dependent rather than uniform. ⚠️ No number is attached; do NOT re-cut the $20-30bn band on this.**
- 🔴🔴 **THE NETWORKING/COPPER ARGUMENT, WITH ITS ONE HARD NUMBER.** Broadcom's **400G-class SerDes** lets customers retain **copper inside AI racks for longer**, which they prefer because copper is *"cheaper, lower-latency and lower-power than optics."* Management's read of rivals: *"competitors discussing NPO/CPO more aggressively partly reflects weaker capabilities, whereas Broadcom can defer the optical transition until it is economically necessary."* **The number: roughly 80% of connectivity within Google's scale-up topology remains COPPER**, with optical already standard for longer-distance scale-out. **Management does not expect CPO to become a major scale-up technology until roughly 2028/29.** ➤ **Set against [[ALAB]] management the same week (NPO *"could begin appearing around 2027"*, CPO *"further out"*) — the two are consistent on direction and roughly consistent on timing, from opposite sides of the trade.** ⚠️ **SELF-SERVING: a copper-advantaged vendor arguing copper lasts longer. The falsifiable part is the 80% figure and the 2028/29 date.**
- 🔴🔴 **SUPPLY — AND ONE LINE HERE SITS AWKWARDLY WITH WHAT THIS PAGE ALREADY CARRIES.** TSMC remains the primary foundry partner and management sees near-term alternatives as limited because the products need *"not only leading-edge digital processes but also mature mixed-signal PDKs developed through years of collaboration."* Consequently **"Samsung discussions are currently primarily MEMORY-RELATED"**, and management **"does not currently view [[INTC]] as having a production-ready process for Broadcom's requirements."** ⚠️⚠️ **FLAGGED AS A POSSIBLE INCONSISTENCY, NOT RESOLVED: this page carries, from JPM's 09-03 callback transcript, [[SAMSUNG]] *"confirmed as a 2nm AND 3nm second-source foundry in 2H next year."* Bernstein's rendering of the same management team three weeks later describes the Samsung relationship as primarily memory. These may be two different agreements (the 07-25 MOU spans both memory and foundry) or they may be a genuine walk-back. BOTH ARE NOW ON THE PAGE; neither is deleted. ➤ This is the single most checkable divergence from this run — resolve it at the December call or the next foundry disclosure.**
- 🔴 **MEMORY AND PACKAGING, BOTH SIZED IN TIME.** Memory is **secured directly by Broadcom through LTAs with costs PASSED THROUGH to customers**, and management believes it has **sufficient memory supply through 2029.** On packaging: Broadcom is building **proprietary advanced-packaging capacity in Malaysia** because architectures are getting harder to manufacture — **2 dies per package today, 4 next year, and potentially 16 the following year** — and **this capacity is for Broadcom's OWN custom-chip customers rather than a merchant packaging business, and will SUPPLEMENT rather than REPLACE CoWoS.** ➤➤ **THE DIE-COUNT LADDER (2 → 4 → 16) IS NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE AND IS THE CLEANEST PHYSICAL JUSTIFICATION YET FOR THE FY28 CONTENT STEP. ✅ The cost pass-through on memory corroborates the `Gross margin / mix` row's standing explanation — dilutive to GM%, neutral to gross profit dollars.**
- 🔭 **AND TWO ARGUMENTS THAT ARE POSITIONING RATHER THAN DATA.** Management **pushed back on the view that NVIDIA-based infrastructure will dominate, including Elon Musk's comments favouring an all-NVIDIA approach**, arguing frontier labs with large, predictable workloads have a strong economic and performance incentive to build custom silicon because they understand their own models. On the software moat: **custom XPUs *"can increasingly support broader PyTorch-based models"*** beyond the workloads they were optimised for, **albeit with the best performance/watt still coming from purpose-built hardware/software co-design.** And a structural claim worth tracking: **closed frontier models are *"structurally better positioned to fund increasingly expensive compute infrastructure than open-weight models."*** ⚠️⚠️ **THIS LAST ONE IS THE DIRECT INVERSE OF WHAT [[NVDA]] MANAGEMENT TOLD THE SAME ANALYST ON THE SAME TOUR — NVIDIA expects *"roughly 80% of models ultimately to be open"* and reads open-model adoption as positive for accelerated computing. Two vendors, one bus tour, opposite readings of the same variable. Logged as the cleanest one-day disagreement of this run; neither adopted.**

- ✅ **READ-THROUGH ONLY, NO AVGO DATAPOINT: Morgan Stanley's *"Powering AI: Racking Up the Power Shortfall"* (Stephen C Byrd / James E Faucette et al, 2026-09-21, [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Morgan_Stanley_AMZN_Powering_AI_Racking_Up_the_Power_Shortfall.html)) names AVGO in its beneficiary list and nowhere else.** Verbatim, in full: *"Broadcom (AVGO) benefits from expanding hyperscaler capex across networking and custom silicon. We expect Broadcom to retain its position as the majority supplier to Google's TPU program, while broader demand for custom accelerators expands the opportunity. We view AVGO as a critical enabler of hyperscaler efforts to increase compute capacity and improve infrastructure economics."* ⚠️ **NO AVGO RATING, PRICE TARGET, ESTIMATE OR SEGMENT NUMBER APPEARS IN THE NOTE — the "majority supplier to Google's TPU program" line is an assertion carried without sizing, and it is NOT a new mark on the `[[GOOG]] share` row. The note's substance is US power (97 GW demand / 57 GW gross / 33 GW net shortfall through 2028) and is owned on [[NVDA]] and [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).**

- 🔴🔴 AND IT BEARS DIRECTLY ON THE PRODUCT-LINE FORK THIS WIKI OPENED THIS MORNING.** The **09-21 notion-ingest** logged JPM's **Harlan Sur** and Jefferies' **Blayne Curtis** describing [[ANTHROPIC]]'s ~10GW as **two incompatible product lines** — Sur has Anthropic on **Google TPU through CY28** with its own ASIC only at architectural-spec stage (which is what keeps AVGO at ~75% of TPU units in 2028), while Curtis has Anthropic already **differentiating away from the Ironwood copy-exact inside the FY28 guide window.** ➤ **Management's own line — *"engaged with every generation going forward"* and *"revenue is stable to growing"* — is CONSISTENT WITH BOTH readings and therefore RESOLVES NEITHER. The falsifiable date remains the 2nm TPU tape-out in 1H27, visible in TSMC planning by early 2027.**
- ✅ THE SUPPLY-LED GATING CLAIM IS CORROBORATED ACROSS THE COMPLEX THE SAME DAY: [[NVDA]] names wafers, CoWoS, memory, substrates and optics as its supply priorities; [[AMAT]] says customer **cleanroom** is the constraint; Jefferies' Seoul trip says **no new memory cleanroom before late 2027/early 2028**; and [[INTC]] management says **substrate yields "aren't great right now."** **Four layers, one week, same conclusion: the binding constraint is physical, not commercial.**

ANTHROPIC IS WELL ON ITS WAY TO AN IPO… OpenAI — we still have less clarity"** | Vital Knowledge: management *"**pushed back on worries it is materially expanding its balance sheet**, noting recent financing deals have been done in partnership w/ sophisticated third-party financial partners"* (2026-09-02). **🆕🔴 SINAL RE-SCORE #3 (2026-09-05, 1 source: CEO Hock Tan verbatim from the Q3 FY26 call, surfaced via @TheTranscript_ corpus 2026-09-04) — ONE ROW GAINS ITS MISSING EXPLANATION AND HOLDS.**

- **`Gross margin / mix` row — HELD at its current signal, but the DRIVER is now stated by management rather than inferred by the desk.** The row carries FQ4 GM guided **~73%, down ~500bp y/y and ~60bp below Street**. Hock's framing: **AI MEMORY CONTENT STRUCTURALLY DILUTES the headline semiconductor gross margin**, and therefore ***"Stop focusing on gross margin is what we're saying, look at where it matters, OPERATING MARGIN at the bottom of the — at the end of the day because the growth in revenue FAR SURPASSES the growth in OpEx… we have a lot of accretion operating leverage."*** ➤ 🔴 **THIS TIES DIRECTLY TO THE `$/GW` ROW AND CLOSES A LOOP THE 09-03/04 RECONCILIATION LEFT OPEN. Broadcom's own silicon was established at ~$13bn/GW blended INCLUDING HBM. Bought-in memory resold inside an XPU is high-dollar, low-margin content — so the SAME mix that makes the $/GW figure large is the mix that compresses gross margin. The two facts are one fact, and management is not deflecting.**
- ⚠️ **THE FALSIFIABLE TEST IS THE SPREAD, NOT THE LEVEL, AND IT SHOULD BE TRACKED FROM FQ4: the claim is that revenue growth outruns OpEx growth by enough to EXPAND operating margin WHILE gross margin falls. Track GM% and OM% together each quarter. If OM% stops expanding while GM% keeps falling, the mix explanation has failed and the correct read becomes PRICING. Nothing else on the table moves.**

Redburn rates Broadcom Buy with no PT in the note.** 🗓️ **And a dated catalyst: a UBS-hosted virtual group NDR on 2026-10-01, 12:30-1:25pm ET, with Hock Tan, Amie Thuener and Ji Yoo — the same roster as the 09-21 Bernstein session — hosted by Timothy Arcuri. ⚠️ Logistics, not research.**

**Síntese do quarter (opened 2026-09-02):** **The print was in line, the FQ4 guide a touch light, the FY27 AI number missed every buy-side bar by $15-25bn, and the FY28 number beat the Street by ~28% — so the stock fell ~3-4% after hours on a call whose CONTENT was, on balance, the most constructive AVGO has given.** Two things genuinely changed: **(1) the [[GOOG]] share-loss fear was answered head-on** (*"never been stronger"*, v8i shipping ahead of [[MEDIATEK]]'s v8T, multi-tens-of-billions annually) — though **not** on the COT/value-capture axis this page opened 08-26; and **(2) the binding constraint moved from demand to supply and was itemised** (foundry · substrates · HBM · land/power/shell), with management stating outright that $115bn is a SECURED-SUPPLY number that can be revised up. **The bear hooks are unchanged and now sharper: the FQ4 operating-margin guide is below Street on the metric management chose to be judged on, and the $/GW arithmetic ($11-12bn implied by Hock's own GW counts vs the $20-30bn he stated) was asked and left unbridged.** ⚠️ **Positioning note for scoring the tape, not the thesis: JPM's desk had AVGO as *"a quite crowded net long — helped over the past few weeks by short covering into earnings"* (Meyers), while the Barclays desk read *"positioning has been leaning short"* (Rand) — two desks, opposite reads, same day. Do not use either as evidence about the fundamentals.** ➕ **UPDATE 2026-09-19 — the $/GW gap the synthesis calls *"asked and left unbridged"* has now been stress-tested from the bull side and it held. An independent author summed Hock's own 2028 customer GW ladder (17GW) and multiplied by the midpoint of the **$20-30bn/GW** he stated, getting **~$425bn of potential 2028 AI revenue against the $230bn guided** — i.e. a sandbagging claim. **On this page's reconciled $11.5bn/GW the identical arithmetic gives ~$196bn, BELOW the guide.** The two answers differ by ~2.2x on the same GW count, which is the cleanest demonstration yet that **the $/GW basis — not the GW ladder — is the live uncertainty on this name.** Separately, the GW ladder itself was corroborated by verbatim quotation this run (see the Sinal row above): the numbers are no longer in doubt; only what a GW is worth to Broadcom is.**

**🆕🔴 SÍNTESE ADDENDUM #4 (2026-09-18, 1 source — New Street Research · Ferragu et al, *"Here we are – $4tn AI capex in 2030?"*; a FORECAST note, no AVGO rating, PT or estimate):** **the quarter's verdict does not move, and no signal row is re-scored — NSR's AVGO inputs ($58bn FY26 / $115bn FY27 / $230bn FY28, 30GW across six XPU customers, >20GW enabled by the financing platform) are this page's own marks read back by an outside house.** **What the note adds is the DENOMINATOR the $230bn has been missing: 2030 XPU spending of $1,702bn with NVIDIA at ~60% leaves ~$680bn of non-NVIDIA XPU value in 2030, and networking compounding faster than compute (63% vs 53% CAGR to $465bn) is the first outside corroboration of management's "networking grows as fast as XPU."** ⚠️ **And it puts AVGO inside the system's credit arithmetic rather than beside it: NSR's $3.7tn external-financing requirement is sized against ~$1.8tn of 2027 EBITDA at hyperscalers + NVDA + AVGO (~2.2x debt/EBITDA) — the `Off-balance-sheet / lab financing` row's open question ("whose balance sheet, and how big") now has an industry-scale answer from outside, while the AVGO-specific disclosure gap it was opened on remains exactly where the print left it.**

**🆕🔴🔴 SYNTHESIS ADDENDUM (2026-09-03, four post-print callbacks — MS/Moore, BofA/Arya, UBS/Winkler, JPM):** **the quarter's debate has moved off "did AVGO lose Google?" and onto three sharper things.** ① **The customer base is being re-based onto two private, pre-IPO frontier labs — JPM has [[ANTHROPIC]] + [[OPENAI]] at 62% of FY27 and 72% of FY28 AI revenue, with [[GOOG]] third by FY28.** The Google-concentration bear is not resolved so much as **replaced by a counterparty-credit bear**, which is why MS's own note lists *"financing concerns and IPO clarity"* as the leading negative. ② **The $/GW basis flipped: four houses now put AVGO content at $10-15bn/GW against a $20-30bn total build cost, contradicting the primary transcript's "our content" wording from 24 hours earlier** — and it matters because the *lower* content number is the one consistent with the $115bn/$230bn guide, i.e. **the guide never implied the higher figure**. ③ **The genuinely new structural claim is that content per GW does NOT grow** — Hock's own mechanism is that rising power per chip means fewer chips per gigawatt, so ASP gains are offset by unit losses — **which puts AVGO in direct, dated opposition to [[NVDA]]'s mid-teens → mid-20s → ~$40bn Vera Rubin content ramp.** ➤ **If Hock is right, AVGO's AI revenue is a pure GW-deployment function and every forecast should be built as GW × ~$13bn, not as a content-inflation story. That is a materially different model from the one most bulls are running, and it is the most testable thing said this week.** ⚠️ **The one number that should NOT be carried forward without challenge is BofA's Google GW decline (4-4.5GW → ~3GW → less), because management declined to rebut it — it is either the most important disclosure of the day or an allocation-math artefact, and nothing in this window settles which.**

**🆕 PRIMARY RECONCILIATION (2026-09-02, /run-inbox — 8-K + Bloomberg LIVE transcript): the synthesis above STANDS, and the primary's first contribution is a negative result worth stating — THERE IS NO RELAY-VS-PRIMARY DISCREPANCY ON THE HEADLINE P&L.** Revenue, GM, op margin, EPS, CFO, FCF, both segments, the AI line, the whole FQ4 guide and the FY26/27/28 AI numbers all reconcile to the company's own 8-K and call. **What the primary changes is at the edges, and in one place it changes how to trade the page's central catalyst: (1) management said outright it does *"NOT intend to update this AI revenue guidance on a QUARTERLY BASIS,"* so the FY27 raise this page flags as a self-declared trigger is not a December event by default — the GS Communacopia slot on 8-Sep is the first realistic venue. (2) The XPV platform moved from press-reported to MANAGEMENT-CONFIRMED (>20GW for [[OPENAI]] + [[ANTHROPIC]] by end-2028, first $35bn tranche closed in June and already deploying), with the company calling its guarantees *"modest"* and *"low risk"* against a disclosed $29bn maximum exposure — an adjective to score, not a number to adopt. (3) Two relay overstatements corrected: TPU v8 is *"comparable if not surpasses"* Vera Rubin, not *"surpasses"*; and *"Tomahawk 6 sold out"* was the JPM analyst's phrase inside his own question, not management's. (4) One relay UNDERSTATEMENT corrected in the bulls' favour: the indium-phosphide laser build is *"MORE THAN TRIPLING year on year"* for two years, not *"already doubled."* (5) The $/GW row gains a THIRD basis — the $30bn is the LABS' revenue per GW, not AVGO's — plus our own (clearly marked) ~12-17GW-shipped-vs-30GW-demanded bridge that nobody on the call made.** ➤ **Net effect on the quarter's verdict: unchanged in direction, better evidenced, and one degree less tradeable on the FY27 raise.**

**🆕🔴🔴 SÍNTESE ADDENDUM #2 (2026-09-03, ★ PRIMARY — the JPM callback TRANSCRIPT, "AVGO post-FQ3 group callback" · Harlan Sur):** **the transcript does not change the quarter's verdict; it changes how much of the guide you should read as a NUMBER versus a CHOICE.** JPM's arithmetic makes $115bn mechanical — **order book $140-150bn × the same 80-85% ship-rate he applies to [[NVDA]] and [[MRVL]] = $115-125bn implied, and AVGO took the BOTTOM of its own band to set up the $230bn FY28** (itself ~85% of a $270-280bn book, against sell-side $180bn and buyside ~$205-210bn). ➤ **On that reading the FY27 "miss" is a management FRAMING decision, not a demand signal, and the tradeable question collapses onto whether supply unlocks — which is precisely where [[SAMSUNG]]'s 2nm/3nm second-sourcing in 2H next year and the Singapore substrate-then-packaging ramp sit.** **Three things this page must NOT tidy up:** ① **the FOUR $/GW bases spoken in this single call** (hardware infrastructure $20-30bn · **AVGO content $11-15bn, ~$13bn mix-adjusted** · [[ANTHROPIC]] ARR ~$30bn · fully-loaded cost ~$5bn/yr) — **and JPM's rendering of the $20-30bn as *hardware infrastructure INCLUDING the chip* is not BofA/UBS/MS's *total system cost including power and cooling*, so the 09-03 contradiction NARROWS without closing**; ② **JPM's own FY27 Google-internal number, which travels from "flat to slightly up" to "10, 12%" to "12 to 15%" inside one call** — the page carries the **10-15% range PLUS the inconsistency**, because an analyst quoting a 0-15% spread in thirty minutes is telling you the line is not held with conviction; ③ **the customer-concentration rotation is a re-basing, not a resolution** — Google internal goes **65% (2026) → 29% (2027) → 19% (2028)** while two private pre-IPO labs take ~73% of FY28 AI revenue. ⚠️ **Label hygiene for anyone reading the 09-03 rows: every "CLT" is an ASR rendering of COT (customer-owned tooling) — same object as the 08-26 MS Asia item, only the label was wrong.** ⚠️ **And one PT hygiene note: the $600 Sur quotes is FAIR VALUE on a client call, not a published target — JPM's PT on this page remains $580.**

**🆕🔴 SÍNTESE ADDENDUM #3 (2026-09-08/09, ★ PRIMARY — the BBG INITIAL DRAFT transcript of the Communacopia fireside; MANAGEMENT):** **the quarter's verdict is unchanged and so is the guide — what moved is the QUALITY of the argument on both sides.** For the bulls, the two structural legs are now described by the CEO himself rather than by press and credit desks: **XPV explicitly *"not circular financing"*, with banks carrying the lab risk and AVGO on residual guarantees only; the next TWO Jalapeño generations already in development; a 2 → 4 → 8-die packaging ladder against an ~800mm² reticle limit as the answer to a Moore's Law that *"has come to an end"*; and a DATED capital-returns decision in December against a record cash balance.** For the bears, the single most quotable thing the CEO said is that **~$20bn of the ~$30bn ARR a gigawatt generates accrues to the MODEL layer, and the remaining ~$10bn is *"fought over"* by, among others, *"the chip and memory guys"*** — i.e. by Broadcom. ➤ **Both sides of this quarter's debate now have a management source. The stock-relevant question the fireside did NOT answer is the one the print left open: the $/GW CONTENT bridge ($20-30bn stated vs $11-12bn implied by his own GW counts). He was not asked it again.**


**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-20 (/wiki-ingest 21h) — THE BUS-TOUR MEETING ADDS THREE THINGS THE F3Q26 CALL DID NOT: **FOUR-COMPUTE-DIE XPUs TARGETED FOR C28 PRODUCTION**, AN EXPLICIT STATEMENT THAT THE **C28 GUIDE CARRIES ONLY THE CONTRACTUAL FLOOR**, AND A DATED [[OPENAI]] JALAPEÑO RAMP — *"EARLY C27, WITH A GEN 2 IN THE WORKS BEHIND THAT."***

(Jefferies · **William Beavington** (Global SEMIS Daily, 2026-09-20) carrying the **US Semis Silicon Valley Bus Tour** — two days of investor meetings with **13 companies** across Compute, Semi-Cap, Memory and Optics, **2026-09-18/19**. ⚠️ **SALES/DESK DISTRIBUTION OF MANAGEMENT MEETING FEEDBACK — no rating, no price target and no estimate change is attached to any company line; these are paraphrases of private management commentary, not company disclosure.**)

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-09-21 (notion-ingest) — TWO ANALYSTS, TWO DAYS APART, BOTH SAY THE FY28 $230bn GUIDE IS TOO LOW — AND THEY GET THERE BY *OPPOSITE* ROUTES ON [[ANTHROPIC]]. SUR HAS ANTHROPIC ON **GOOGLE TPU THROUGH CY28** (WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY ITS 10GW COUNTS AS *TPU UNITS* AND AVGO'S TPU UNIT SHARE PRINTS ~75% IN 2028). CURTIS HAS ANTHROPIC'S DESIGNS **ALREADY MOVING OFF THE IRONWOOD RACK COPY TOWARD CUSTOM** (WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY *"HALF THEIR GIGAWATTS"* CARRY NO [[MEDIATEK]] RISK). ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT AVERAGE THEM. THE FORK IS DATED AND TESTABLE, AND IT POINTS THE SAME WAY FOR AVGO REVENUE BUT THE OPPOSITE WAY FOR [[GOOG]]'s EXTERNAL-TPU LINE.**

(Two Capstone-attended calls, both **Notion AI machine transcripts of unlabelled multi-speaker rooms** — speaker turns unmarked, names and numbers carry garble risk. **① Harlan Sur + Mayur Ramdami (J.P. Morgan), Semiconductor Fall Series webinar, 2026-09-17** — Capstone notes (Notion transcript), [source](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-09-17_JPMorgan_Semiconductor-Fall-Series-update-Sur-Ramdami.md). This is the **SPOKEN** companion to the Fall Series **deck** already ingested 09-17/09-18 (see `themes/custom-asic-tpu.md`) — the deck and the webinar are the same vintage, and the webinar carries the Q&A the deck does not. **② Blayne Curtis (Jefferies), semis bus-tour recap call, 2026-09-18** — Capstone notes (Notion transcript), [source](../_equity_calls/Semis/2026-09-18_Jefferies_semis-bus-tour-recap-call-Curtis.md). This is the **SPOKEN** companion to the Jefferies **written** bus-tour recap (Beavington, 09-20) in the block above, and it **resolves the debrief-call catalyst this page flagged on 09-16**. ⚠️ **Neither call carries a rating change, a price-target change or an estimate revision.**)

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE FORK, STATED BOTH WAYS SO IT CAN BE SCORED LATER.** **Sur (JPM, 09-17):** *"Anthropic will use Google's TPU ASIC to calendar 28… and then potentially start to ramp their own ASIC co-designed by Broadcom in calendar 29, calendar 30"* — Broadcom and Anthropic are *"in the early phases of the ARCHITECTURAL SPECIFICATION"* of Anthropic's first-generation ASIC. **Curtis (Jefferies, 09-18):** *"today Anthropic is buying the Google RAC copy exact for Ironwood, but… the designs will become more DIFFERENTIATED. And it does sound like the trend is to CUSTOM there and NOT USING A TPU. So that was a huge data point. And it kind of takes, okay, well then HALF their gigawatts are not going to even have the risk of MediaTek."* ➤➤ **Both are bullish for AVGO's FY28 dollars, and for opposite reasons — Sur because Anthropic's 10GW stays inside the *TPU* line Broadcom dominates, Curtis because it LEAVES the TPU line for an Anthropic-specific part. ⚠️ THEY CANNOT BOTH BE RIGHT ABOUT THE FY28 WINDOW, and the difference is worth ~10GW of product-line attribution. ➤ THE TEST: whether Anthropic's 2028 silicon is described as a TPU SKU or as an Anthropic part — watch the FY27 tape-outs and the F4Q26 (9-Dec-2026) call. ➤ FOR [[GOOG]] THE SIGN FLIPS: on Sur, Anthropic is Google-TPU demand through 2028; on Curtis it is walking off the TPU line inside the guide window. Cross-filed [[GOOG]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **JPM RE-STATES THE APRIL AGREEMENT IN ITS STRONGEST FORM YET — AND TRIMS ITS OWN UNIT-SHARE NUMBERS AT BOTH ENDS WHILE DOING IT.** Sur: *"the Google Broadcom Revenue Supply Agreement signed in April… includes increasing TPU revenues committed to Broadcom over the next FIVE years [and] a LOCK-IN ON THE NEXT FOUR GENERATIONS… Broadcom will retain MAJORITY TPU unit share and revenue share [including] Google's most strategic TPU SKUs"*, and *"Broadcom will retain the bigger unit share and revenue share of the TPU programs at Google THROUGH 2030."* Asked directly how long the relationship holds: *"Broadcom is not getting phased out of Google anytime over the next, call it FIVE TO SEVEN YEARS."* ✅ **Nothing here is new in kind — the five-year/four-generation (V8-V11) LTA has been on this page since June and was re-stated on the 09-03 JPM callback. What is new is the *through-2030* horizon attached to unit AND revenue share, and the *"most strategic SKUs"* qualifier.** ⚠️ **THESIS DRIFT, LOGGED NOT SMOOTHED: the 09-03 callback had AVGO at *~two-thirds of total TPU units in 2027 and 75-80% in 2028*. The 09-17 webinar says **60% in 2027 and ~75% in 2028** — the same analyst, two weeks later, a TRIM at both ends. Old values moved to `## Changelog`; both marks stay attributed.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE GW→UNITS TRIANGULATION, WITH ITS ARITHMETIC AMBIGUITY FLAGGED RATHER THAN RESOLVED.** Sur's method, offered as the *answer to "how does the market get comfortable if the supply chain can't see the volumes"*: take Broadcom's own disclosed GW by customer, convert at the known 2nm ASP, and back out units. **2027: [[ANTHROPIC]] 5GW + [[GOOG]] internal 3GW (JPM's own assumption, NOT a company figure) → *"that gets me 6 million"* TPU units for Broadcom against a total (internal + external) of *"nine and a half million TPUs… that Google will ship"* → 6.0/9.5 = 63%, which Sur rounds to *"60% of the TPU unit volume."* 2028: ANTHROPIC 10GW (*"that's ALL TPU"*) + GOOG internal 4GW = 14GW → *"it roughly equates to about nine and a half million TPUs"* → ~75% unit share.** ⚠️⚠️ **THE 9.5m IS USED TWICE IN THE SAME ANSWER — once as the 2027 INDUSTRY TOTAL and once as the 2028 BROADCOM figure — and the two readings do not reconcile against JPM's own published unit table (Hariharan's revised CoWoS table, 09-17, has TOTAL Google TPU units at 9.5m in 2027 and 11.4m in 2028; 9.5m Broadcom units at 75% share would require a ~12.7m 2028 total). ✅ **The 2027 leg reconciles EXACTLY to the house table (9.5m total, 6m Broadcom, 63%≈60%). The 2028 leg does not.** ➤ **CARRY THE SHARES (60% / ~75%) AND THE GW INPUTS. DO NOT CARRY 9.5m AS A 2028 BROADCOM UNIT COUNT until the deck or a note confirms it.** ⚠️ **GW BASIS GUARD: these are XPU/TPU *deployment* GW on Broadcom's own customer ladder — the same basis as the F3Q26 call. They are NOT facility GW, NOT IT-load GW, and NOT the 800V subset. Never net or chain them against the Jefferies 90GW *total data-centre power* figure below. The four-basis $/GW guard on this page and on [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md) still governs.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **THE ADVANCED-PACKAGING STRATEGY GETS DATES, A PARTNER AND A CAPACITY SPLIT — AND IT IS THE STRUCTURAL REASON SUR TELLS CLIENTS TO DISCOUNT ASIA CHECKS.** Sur: Broadcom has spent *"12 to 18 months"* converting its long-standing **Singapore fab from optical-component manufacturing (EML/CW lasers) to an advanced-packaging facility**, driven by advanced substrates being *"one of the biggest bottlenecks."* Concretely: a **strategic JV partnership in Japan (transcribed *"Topan"* — read Toppan, ⚠️ name unverified)**; **advanced-substrate manufacturing kicked off *"literally this month"* — September 2026**; **one-third of Broadcom's advanced-substrate capability will sit in Singapore**, *"only going to be relegated to their most performant, most complex ASIC programs"*; **[[AMAT]] has set up an advanced-packaging R&D facility *"literally right next door"***, and over 12-18 months the two build *"a full blown advanced packaging [architecture] Broadcom will bring to the market STARTING IN 2028… it basically REPLACES THE ENTIRE TSMC CoWoS and everything else that happens after that."* The claim: solutions *"a half step to one step AHEAD of TSMC and Intel."* ✅ **Consistent with, and far more specific than, the 09-02 call's *"Singapore substrate plant deployed in FY27"* and UBS's 07-27 read that the Samsung deal folds packaging into AVGO's own Singapore site.** ⚠️ **This is JPM's characterisation of company plans, not a Broadcom disclosure, and the *"half-step to one-step ahead of TSMC"* claim is an assertion with no benchmark attached.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE END-GAME DIE COUNT — AND IT SITS FAR ABOVE TWO NUMBERS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE.** Sur: *"their ultimate end game over the next call it 18 to 24 months is to be able to bring to market an advanced package that can accommodate **16 COMPUTE DIES** in a single package with **16 to 18 HBM STACKS**… I don't think anybody will have that sort of integration capability."* ⚠️⚠️ **CONFLICT, LOGGED AND NOT NETTED. (a) Hock Tan's own ladder (GS Communacopia, 09-08, ★ PRIMARY) is **2 → 4 → 8 dies** against an ~800mm² reticle limit. (b) Jefferies' bus-tour meeting feedback (Beavington, 09-20, block above) has ***"four compute die XPUs targeted for C28 production."*** (c) Sur's 16 dies + 16-18 HBM stacks is 2-4x either of them on an 18-24 month horizon. **The CEO's own number is the conservative anchor; Sur's is a roadmap ambition relayed second-hand. Treat 16 as an aspiration, 4-8 as the underwritable range, and note that the HBM-stack count is the line that matters for [themes/hbm-memory](themes/hbm-memory.md) if it is ever confirmed.**

- 🔴🔴 **WHY THE ASIA CHECKS MISS IT — AND THE FALSIFIABLE DATE THAT COMES WITH IT.** Sur: *"a large part of Broadcom's supply chain will be INTERNAL and masked from these so-called Asia supply chain checks… [the 2nm TPU] is not going to show up on any TSMC CoWoS checks or Intel EMIB-T checks."* His caution on how the sell-side builds unit forecasts is the useful part: *"mostly what you see out of my Asia counterparts is a CoWoS update… then they take those CoWoS numbers from TSMC and translate that into 'here's the number of XPU units that XYZ is going to ship.'"* ➤➤ **THE TESTABLE CLAIM, AND IT IS NEAR-DATED: the 2nm TPU *"is going to TAPE OUT IN THE FIRST HALF OF NEXT YEAR"* and *"will show up in TSMC's planning systems either END OF THIS YEAR, BEGINNING OF NEXT YEAR"*, ramping in CY28. If Asia desks are still saying *"we don't see any Broadcom 2nm"* in Q1 2027, Sur's frame is wrong. Add to `## Catalysts`.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE 336G SerDes STORY IS RE-TOLD WITH THE CAUSALITY INVERTED VS WHAT THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED SINCE JUNE.** Sur: the Google CLT team with [[MEDIATEK]] is moving *"from 200 gig to 336 gig… primarily because the CLT team does not have 400 gig SerDes capability… they've had to DIAL BACK the performance of the networking connectivity to 336 gig"*, which then forces *"the entire networking connectivity that sits around that to be in sync with that 336 gig, which we hear is creating a lot of TURMOIL amongst some of their networking and networking connectivity customers."* Broadcom's own roadmap stays Ethernet-aligned: **TPU V8 at 200G today → 400G at 2nm**. Execution colour on CLT: the first-gen V8 *"went through like four or five design iterations and a little bit of delays,"* with *"similar issues continuing on the V9 version."* ⚠️⚠️ **DIRECT COLLISION WITH BEAR ITEM (2026-06-24, FinTwit) ON THIS PAGE, WHICH READS THE SAME 336G NUMBER AS A MEDIATEK *WIN* — MediaTek taking TPU v9 primary orders by *delivering* a custom 336G solution after Broadcom's 448G *missed the deadline*. Same number, opposite agent: a MediaTek capability in one telling, a MediaTek limitation in the other. BOTH REMAIN ON THE PAGE. Neither is adopted.**

- 🔴🔴 **A NET-NEW REVENUE LINE AT GOOGLE THAT IS NOT TPU — AND IT CONTRADICTS A BEAR ITEM ALREADY IN `## Debate`.** Sur: top-of-rack scale-OUT *"will continue to use Tomahawk"*; on scale-UP, Google has used proprietary **ICI + OCS** and *"they're going to try to FORCE FIT that to a 336 gig type of speed"* — whereas on the inferencing roadmap *"Google is going to potentially TRANSITION… to a scale-up architecture that is more ETHERNET-LIKE and less of their proprietary architecture… In addition to TPU for inferencing, **Broadcom is working with Google on a CUSTOM ETHERNET SCALE-UP SILICON opportunity as well.**"* And the strategic ranking: *"the one that you want to always keep your eye on is the INFERENCING roadmap, which IS the Broadcom roadmap, because inferencing is where the higher TPU volumes are and where the most performant TPUs will be."* ⚠️ **This cuts against the 2026-09-09 broker-unconfirmed claim carried in `## Debate` that *"TPU rack does not use scale-up from Broadcom"* and that [[ALAB]]/[[CRDO]] are the scale-up-levered names. Logged as a two-sided item: Sur is describing a FUTURE transition and an OPPORTUNITY, not shipping content — it does not retire the 09-09 point for the current rack.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE SHIPMENT LADDER AND THE TAM, RESTATED ON THE CALL:** *"We estimate Broadcom shipped **$20 billion** in AI ASICs and networking last year. We'll ship around **$60 billion this year**. **Backlog for next year supports $140 billion**, but will be somewhat limited by SUPPLY CONSTRAINTS."* High-end ASIC market *"a **$70 billion** market this year"* with AVGO #1 and [[MRVL]] #2. ⚠️⚠️ **BASIS: the $140bn is what Sur says the BACKLOG SUPPORTS, against the company's own supply-secured FY27 AI guide of $115bn and JPM's own published FY27 model line of ~$135bn (Fall Series deck, per [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md)). Three different numbers for three different things — backlog capacity, house model, company guide. NEVER present them as a range.** ✅ **The $70bn TAM matches the deck's *"~$60-70B in CY26"* custom-AI-ASIC sizing; the deck's separate ~$68bn ASIC bucket is a DIFFERENT perimeter (it includes [[AAPL]] A19 and Nokia RAN) — scope trap already documented on the theme page.**

- 🔴 **NETWORKING, DATED:** **Tomahawk 6** (102T, 3nm) started shipping 1H26, *"already SOLD OUT for next year"*, with **Samsung being brought up as a second source to unlock more 3nm supply from 2H27**; **Tomahawk 7** *"recently taped out"* at **2nm, 200 terabits per chip, 512 channels of 400G SerDes in a single package**, on an *"aggressive two-year cadence."*

- 🔴 **THE PIPELINE SLIDE, SPOKEN — AND IT PUTS *ARKE* ON BROADCOM, WHICH IS THE PAGE'S OPEN CONFLICT.** [[META]]: *"a total of FIVE programs"* — two 3nm ramping 2026, **Arke (transcribed "ARC") at 2nm ramping 1H27**, **Astrid (2nm) 2H27**, **Apollo 2028**. [[OPENAI]]: first-gen **Jalapeño** *"on track to ramp calendar 27."* **SoftBank/ARM**: first-gen XPU ASIC also CY27. Up-and-comers: **SambaNova (transcribed "Samanova") with five generations of XPU ASICs, Furiosa AI, Etched**. ⚠️ **ARKE OWNERSHIP: this is now the THIRD JPM voice (Sur 09-17, deck 09-17/18, specialist sales 09-15) placing Arke inside AVGO's Meta pipeline, against the [[MEDIATEK]] page which has carried Arke as a MediaTek programme since June. The conflict opened 2026-09-15 and is still OPEN — logged, not resolved here.**

- 🔴🔴🔴 **JEFFERIES: $230bn IS *"A VERY CONSERVATIVE NUMBER"* AND THE PATH TO $400bn IS ARITHMETIC, NOT HOPE.** Curtis's build: *"The math was **10 gigawatts for Anthropic, five for OpenAI, call it Meta two. It left THREE for Google.**"* On Google: *"they only included the **CONTRACTUALLY OBLIGATED** amount for Google… I've since heard that that three is probably TOO CONSERVATIVE and the contractual obligated number is higher than that… that three or four gigawatts could be DOUBLED."* And two further handicaps he says are inside the guide: *"that includes handicapping the SECOND HALF OF FISCAL 28 for anything that might slip"* and *"they made sure that a customer's… financing were REMOVED."* The bull case: *"increase the 20 gigawatts to 25 — **it's not going to be 40, they don't have capacity** … $10 per gigawatt closer to **15 plus** and 25 gigawatts, you'll get there [to $400bn]. So that's **$45 plus bucks of earnings**. It makes Avago CHEAPER THAN NVIDIA. And I think people would want to play the SHARE GAINER, not the share loser."* At 30% incremental margins. ➤ **Against the Capstone house model on this page (2028E GW by client: Google 4.8 / Anthropic 7.0 / OpenAI 4.3 / Meta 2.9 = **19.6GW total**), Curtis's base 20GW is a near-match in TOTAL but a very different MIX — the house is ~2GW light on Anthropic and ~2GW heavy on Google versus his contractual-floor split. That mix difference is the whole MediaTek-risk question.**

- ⚠️⚠️🔴 **THE $/GW BASIS TRAP INSIDE THE SAME CALL — READ THIS BEFORE QUOTING ANY OF THE ABOVE.** Curtis states the run-rate as *"In '27, and the numbers they put out for '27, it was **11 and a half billion per gigawatt**. They **kept that FLAT in the 28 forecast**"*, and separately the bull case as *"**15 billion times 25, which is the 400**"* (= $375bn + ~$25bn non-AI). **But the call's auto-summary renders the base case as *"20 gigawatts at $10/gigawatt, plus $30 billion non-AI"* = $230bn — i.e. it treats $230bn as TOTAL company revenue. The company's own F3Q26 guide is $230bn of AI SEMICONDUCTOR revenue, which on 20GW implies exactly $11.5bn/GW and reconciles to Curtis's own spoken FY27 figure.** ➤➤ **USE: 20GW × ~$11.5bn/GW = $230bn AI. The *"$10/GW + $30bn non-AI"* rendering is the machine summary's arithmetic and does NOT reconcile to the company guide — the transcript is the record. ⚠️ And note the bull case's $15bn/GW is on the SAME basis as $11.5bn (AVGO AI content per deployed GW) — it is NOT the ~$30bn/GW MODEL-LAYER revenue Hock quoted at Communacopia, and NOT the $20-30bn/GW silicon-plus-systems figure. Four bases already documented; this adds no fifth, but it is the easiest one to mis-chain.**

- 🔴🔴 **WHY $/GW RISES — THE MECHANISM, NAMED BY CUSTOMER.** Curtis: *"we know that like say the **Jalapeño chip has a HIGH-TEENS dollars per gigawatt**. And I think Google is going to move up. **Meta is moving up in a big way** — they're going from these rank-and-recommendation engines that are like a few thousand dollars to like **very HBM-heavy chips**. And also **networking content's going up a ton as they do more custom**. So… even 15 billion times 25 could prove conservative."* ✅ **This is the sourced mechanism behind the 09-03 Jefferies line already on this page (*"sees room ABOVE $230bn as the $/GW moves higher"*) — that note asserted it; this call explains it with three named drivers.**

- ⚠️ **AND CURTIS'S OWN CAVEAT ON THE SHARE FIGHT, WHICH HE SAYS WILL NOT BE SETTLED BY EITHER SIDE:** *"the biggest concern is MediaTek. And you can listen to what Broadcom says and I've said, but you can talk to, you know, **25 MediaTek analysts who say something exactly the opposite. That's not going to get settled.**"* ➤ **Read with the Asia-vs-US desk framing already on this page (08-31): the TPU share fight is a desk-geography fight, not a house-vs-house one. Curtis is the second US analyst in a week to say it out loud.**

- 🔽 **VALUATION / POSITIONING, FOR CONTEXT.** Curtis on the group: *"if you look at the upside case on these AI compute names, many of them are trading at **10 times or below**. And I think it's just too low."* AVGO stays his **top pick** — *"we laid out a 28 case three months ago and said DON'T BUY IT NOW. But this is what to come back to in the FALL. If anything, I think that story is BETTER"* — while conceding *"we moved aside because 27 STALLED OUT like a lot of names."* ⚠️ **No rating or PT is stated on either call.**

  🗓️ **DATED CATALYST ADDED: Broadcom virtual group NDR, Thursday 1 October 2026, 12:30–1:25pm ET, interactive virtual Zoom, "By Request", request deadline Sept 25th 1pm ET. Management: Hock Tan (CEO), Amie Thuener (CFO), Ji Yoo (Head of IR) — the same roster as the 09-21 Bernstein session. UBS research host: Timothy Arcuri (US Semiconductors and Semiconductor Equipment). Capstone has asked its UBS sales rep to be included.** ⚠️ **LOGISTICS, NOT RESEARCH: no thesis, no estimate, no new call is contained in the invite and none has been manufactured from it.**
  🔴 **THE ONE SUBSTANTIVE NEW DATAPOINT — THE SHARPEST TPU UNIT SPLIT THIS PAGE HAS CARRIED: UBS's APAC team now forecasts *"Google ramping 3.5mn units at BROADCOM and 4mn units at MEDIATEK in '27E for TPU v8 and then upgrading to a 4 die structure for MediaTek's v9 and a STITCHED TOGETHER 4 DIE VERSION FOR BROADCOM in 2028"* — ~47% of v8 programme units to AVGO in '27E.** ⚠️⚠️ **NOT NETTED AGAINST THE EXISTING MARKS, AND THE REASON IS BASIS, NOT DIPLOMACY: this is a v8-PROGRAMME unit split for a single calendar year. Sunny Lin's 91% → 62% is an ALL-TPU unit share (2026/27E), MS's ~80% is an ASP/mix share, JPM's ~75% is a 2028 figure, GS's is a 50/50 next-gen split, and MediaTek's own claim is ">50% of the programme". At least three different bases sit in that list. A programme-level split does not read across to an all-generation share and has not been used to move any of them.** ✅ **The offsetting half is genuinely new: UBS has AVGO carrying its OWN distinct 2028 architecture — a stitched four-die part alongside MediaTek's four-die v9 — which rebuts the hardest form of the share-cede case (that AVGO is designed out after v8) and is consistent with management's 09-21 line to Bernstein that "MediaTek participates in pieces of the TPU opportunity but does not provide the same end-to-end architecture."** 🔭 **And the pool is growing faster than the share is falling: Google ASIC racks 59,155 ('26E) → 133,465 ('27E), a 22% CAGR '25-30 and the largest ASIC line in UBS's table; total accelerator (GPU+ASIC) units +32%/+40% YoY to 17.5mn/24.9mn in '26E/'27E; total DIES 28.3mn/46.6mn, +44%/+65%. ⚠️ Dies ≠ chipsets ≠ racks — three bases, never chained.**
  🔭 **THREE DATED, PHYSICAL CORROBORATIONS, NONE OF WHICH MOVES A NUMBER: OpenAI Jalapeno begins SUBSTRATE AND CCL PROCUREMENT IN Q426; the next Meta ramp is LOADED FOR H227; Microsoft's MTIA 200 is extended on GUC before transitioning to MTIA 300 designed by [[MRVL]] at the end of next year.**
  🔭 **FROM REDBURN, A PACKAGING-ARCHITECTURE READ-THROUGH ONLY: "Broadcom ASICs — Varies. Broadcom 3.5D XDSiP process uses HB", with BESI's June 2026 Investor Day reiterating that Broadcom "adopted 3DIC technology with TSMC for its custom ASIC customers", and "Nvidia/Broadcom CPO — Yes, ramps in scale-up networking in 2028/29" (direct Cu-Cu PIC-to-EIC bonding required for 200Gbit/s per lane).** ⚠️ **Redburn's own caveat is the load-bearing half and is carried verbatim: the architectures of Google TPU, OpenAI Jalapeno and AWS Trainium are "closely guarded, so there is little way to independently verify where HB has yet been adopted." A process-CAPABILITY statement, not evidence about any shipping part. For scale, Redburn's entire custom-chip hybrid-bonding bucket is 34 tools by 2030 (28 for ASIC accelerators at 8 bonds per chip) against TSMC's 448 and Intel's 118 — ASIC packaging is a small slice even in a note that assumes universal eventual adoption. Redburn rates Broadcom Buy (footnote); no PT in the note.**

All published results are on **A0 silicon 9 months into the program**; a **B0 stepping is already in the fab with *"roughly a 25% perf-per-watt improvement"***, delivering **13.4 PFLOPs MXFP4 on a single reticle-sized N3P compute die at 700W TDP** (vs **17.5 PFLOPs of dense NVFP4 for a similar-size Rubin compute die on the same node at 900–1,150W**). **HBM4 at 15.4TB/s per package** — *"implies its HBM4 can hit 10Gbps pin speeds, which would give it a slight edge over the 9.6Gbps Nvidia is getting out of its HBM4 in Rubin"*; *"The HBM is likely provided by [[SAMSUNG]]."* 🔴 **THE AVGO NETWORKING CONTENT, WHICH IS THE PART THIS PAGE HAS BEEN MISSING — the scale-up fabric is Broadcom silicon end to end: each rack carries 8 "Chana" switch trays, *"six … for the local domain, which come with one 102.4T Tomahawk 6 switch ASIC each"*, plus 2 global trays that SemiAnalysis thinks could be *"2x 102.4T Tomahawk 6 switches making up to 204.8T per switch tray"*.** Rack topology: 16 "Vindaloo" trays × 8 ASICs = **128 Jalapeño per rack**, local all-to-all over a copper backplane *"just like that of Nvidia's Oberon"*, global domain of **2,048 XPUs across 16 racks** (copper backplane + 1.6T transceivers + optical circuit switch, 8 rails); **~160kW for the two-rack (host + ASIC) system, "basically a double-wide GB300 rack"**; system-level design *"done in partnership with Celestica"*; host "Katsu" trays run **2× [[AMD]] Turin-class EPYC + 1.5TB DRAM**. **Timing: *"production is currently scheduled to gradually ramp over 2027 with most of the output currently scheduled for Q4 of next year"*; *"The next goal is 100MW."*** ⚠️ **THE OFFSET, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE MARGIN ARGUMENT AND IT COMES FROM A BULL: on perf/TCO Jalapeño and Vera Rubin are *"head-to-head, producing almost the same number of output tokens per $"*, and SemiAnalysis attributes part of that to price rather than physics — *"part of this TCO advantage comes from trading Nvidia's high margins for Broadcom's lower (though still high) margins."* Also, sizing the switching opportunity honestly: *"scale-up networking is only about 10% of total system cost."*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_25_ENC_OpenAI_Jalape%C3%B1o_Better_Than_Nvidia_Blackwell_email.html) · [archived (truncated) PDF copy](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_on_Jalapeno.html) |

Never compare or chain it with the $10–20bn/GW (Hock's own range) or Jefferies' $11bn/GW on this page. Same discipline already applied to Epoch's $34.5bn ÷ ">1 GW".** 🆕 **NET-NEW #1 — THE GUARANTEE'S PROPER NAME AND ITS RUN-OFF: *"Broadcom, in effect, **guaranteed the two senior tranches** by agreeing to cover any shortfall if Anthropic stopped paying and the SPV could not sell the hardware for enough to make the senior investors whole. The arrangement, known as **RESIDUAL VALUE SUPPORT**, covers about **$30bn of the $35bn financing**, with **Broadcom's exposure declining as Anthropic makes its lease payments**."*** ➜ **Use "residual value support" as the term of art. Combined with Epoch's draw schedule (~16 stages, ~$24bn out by summer 2027), the exposure profile is a HUMP peaking around mid-2027 — building with the draw, running off with the lease — not a plateau at the $29bn cap.** ✅ **Reconciles, does not replace: FT "~$30bn of the $35bn financing" · Epoch "$30bn of the $34.5bn of debt" · AVGO's disclosed "$29bn maximum exposure." One arrangement, three resolutions — $35bn is the SPV's PURCHASE PRICE, $34.5bn the DEBT, $29bn the disclosed CAP.** 🆕 **NET-NEW #2 — WHY BROADCOM IS IN THE MIDDLE AT ALL, quoted: *"none of the companies involved wanted to carry tens of billions of dollars of AI chips on their balance sheets. Google is already funding record capital spending, so **Broadcom agreed to buy the AI hardware from it. But Broadcom, too, would rather deploy its capital elsewhere.** 'Broadcom is the financing provider, but they don't want to be in the financing business.'"* Resolution: *"**Morgan Stanley helped arrange a private-credit vehicle**… in an adaptation of the **vendor-financing model BOEING AND GE built to sell aircraft and engines**."* **The Boeing/GE analogy is the right mental model for this exposure and is net-new here; MS as arranger is also net-new (Epoch named Apollo/Blackstone/global banks as the capital).** FT's risk split: *"Google guarantees the data centres. **Broadcom commits to buying the chips and helps finance them.** Apollo and Blackstone provide much of the private-credit capital."*** 🆕 **NET-NEW #3 — THE FIRST TRANCHE, DATED AND UNIT-COUNTED: *"In **JUNE**, the first tranche of TPU hardware passed **from Google THROUGH Broadcom** into this financing blender. A special-purpose vehicle known as **Compute SPV paid $35bn for roughly 1GW** of the AI hardware, representing **around 1MN TPUs**."* The pass-through routing is the mechanical reason the commitments appear on Broadcom's books.** ⚠️🔴 **QUANTITY DISCIPLINE — FOUR NUMBERS, NONE SUPERSEDING ANOTHER: ① **>$150bn of CHIPS** supplied to Anthropic; ② **~$200bn of CONTRACT NOTIONAL**, *"roughly four-fifths tied to the chips themselves"* (≈$160bn chip leg); ③ **~$50bn of DEBT RAISED** (Epoch, 08-12); ④ **$35bn PAID** for the first ~1GW. **The FT's larger figures do NOT overwrite Epoch's ~$50bn — chips and contracts vs debt.** For this page ①/② are the hardware-value statement (product moving through Broadcom) and ③ is the financing quantum; the $128bn of filed commitments is the audited slice.** **CONTEXT, FOR SYMMETRY: Google's counterpart exposure is also now sized — *"backstopped 10 developments with 2.4GW of power… on the hook for as much as $44bn if all the leases go bad, though it marks the liability at $815mn."* Epoch's conclusion (financing is not the binding constraint PROVIDED suppliers absorb part of the risk) is now quantified on both legs: AVGO ~$29-30bn on chips, GOOG ~$44bn notional on datacentres.** ⚠️ **THE BEAR FRAMING, from the source: *"the risk is big if concentrated: $200bn of contracts tied to Anthropic's ability to pay,"* and Jefferies' Jonathan Petersen — *"There's a whole world that's been built underneath those companies, and if their appetite to invest decreases, all of it sees a slowdown. That's the big macro risk."*** **Background: TPUs are *"AI chips [Google] has co-developed with Broadcom since 2016,"* sold in *"pods"* — *"server racks taller than a person that connect thousands of chips into a single computing system."*** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Inside_Googles_200bn_Wall_Street_finance_machine_for_Anthropic.html) |

The primary tells you WHERE, and it is narrower and more actionable than the relay implied: it is TEST capacity, not foundry, and it is a PUSH-OUT, not a cut.** 🔴 **Verbatim, and this datapoint is not anywhere on this page: "from our supply chain check, we see Broadcom (ASR: 'Borcom') is less bullish in expanding capacity, less bullish in expanding capacity. So Broadcom previously, they plan to add more capacity in the second half. But it seems like the plan is pushed out."** Pressed by an analyst on whether that reflects a Samsung 2nm/advanced-packaging dual-source rather than weak demand, Shang **narrows it himself: "when I said Broadcom is becoming less bullish in expanding capacity, I was talking about THE TESTING CAPACITY. I was talking about the testing capacity. But from the foundry side, I think Broadcom will still use TSMC."** ⚠️ **Two things follow. (a) This is a check-based read on a capacity decision, not a statement about orders — but test capacity is the last thing you add before you ship, so a 2H26 test-capacity push-out is the earliest hard-asset tell the supply chain gives on a 2027 volume expectation. Treat any AVGO-linked test-house guide as at risk (cf. [[NVDA]]'s KYEC guide-down on the same call, from the same mechanism running one generation ahead). (b) It also CUTS AGAINST the page's own 07-28 [[KLAC]]-call datapoint that Broadcom is being qualified at [[SAMSUNG]] Foundry 2nm/3nm — Fubon says "I'm not so sure on that" and keeps Broadcom at TSMC on the foundry side. Both are second-hand; hold them as an open disagreement, not a resolution.** The foundry backdrop he does commit to is a constraint either way: **TSMC 2nm and 3nm supply "will be very, very tight" in 2027**, with TSMC capex rising to **$80-85bn in 2027 from $60-64bn in 2026** and Shang explicitly unsure that is enough — "I'm not sure that is enough to support all the customers demand." 🔴 **On V10, the asymmetry of information is itself the datapoint: "currently, we haven't seen MediaTek's ([[MEDIATEK]]) V10 design. But from our checks, we have already seen Broadcom's V10 design. And it is a gigantic chip with a 8 [2]nm compute dies (ASR: 'a 8 to nanometer compute dies' — read as eight 2nm compute dies, consistent with this page's existing '8 N2 compute dies' mark). And we also heard the TDP of this chip will become 6,000 WATTS, 6,000 watts. And it will also adopt 400 or 448G SerDes (ASR: 'services')."** **The 6,000W TDP is net-new to this page** and is a striking number in its own right — for scale, this page's own AMD Helios read has a whole 72-GPU rack at ~240kW, so a single 6kW accelerator package implies rack- and cooling-level co-design well beyond the current generation. **On SerDes the primary forces a distinction this page must keep clean: Fubon says "Broadcom's 400G is ready" TODAY — which was the explicit basis of his PRIOR view that "in the Google TPU V10, [MediaTek] will probably lose some market share back to Broadcom." That view is now WEAKENED, not by anything Broadcom did, but by MediaTek's earnings-call statement that its own 400G-or-higher SerDes will be ready in 2H27 ("a huge surprise to us"), one year earlier than Fubon assumed — see [MEDIATEK.md](MEDIATEK.md).** ⚠️ **Do NOT net Fubon's "Broadcom's 400G is ready" against this page's standing datapoint that AVGO's **448G** SerDes missed the v9 production deadline (MediaTek delivered 336G). Those are different speed grades and the page should carry both: 400G ready, 448G late.** **Finally, the 2028 expectations bar and the bear question, with Fubon's answer — which declines the bull case as much as the bear: asked about broker 2028 custom-ASIC revenue estimates "ranging somewhere between 35 billion to 52 billion" and whether business could "go back to Broadcom, or indeed Google could change the business model and go like the Alchip or GUC route and do it themselves," Shang's answer is neither: "I think INTEL'S PRODUCTION IS THE BIGGEST RISK… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some downside."** ⚠️ **Read that carefully as an AVGO holder: the mechanism by which the $35-52bn 2028 numbers get cut is a TSMC capacity wall, not a reallocation of sockets back to Broadcom. Fubon is not offering AVGO share recapture as the offset — the offset he describes is the whole 2028 TPU number coming down. Vendor-reallocation is explicitly not his swing factor.** Reconfirmed without change: **Tomahawk 6 as one of ASE's three named programs** behind the FoCoS raise to 50k wpm (with [[NVDA]] Vera and [[AMD]] Venice). Morgan · Gokul Hariharan — the GUC/MediaTek upgrades name Broadcom's business model as the thing being dismantled** | competicao | **bear** | ⚠️ **★ THE MOST DIRECT STRUCTURAL ATTACK ON AVGO'S ASIC ECONOMICS PUBLISHED THIS QUARTER, and it is stated as a description of [[GOOG]]'s procurement strategy rather than as a Broadcom call: "we believe that through the evolution from TPUv8 to v10 families, THE BLACK-BOX MODEL FROM US FABLESS VENDOR (WHERE IP, DESIGN SERVICE, MANUFACTURING AND PACKAGING VALUE ADD ARE ALL BUNDLED TOGETHER AT HIGH MARGINS) WILL BE BROKEN DOWN SYSTEMATICALLY IN FAVOR OF A DISAGGREGATED APPROACH"** (JPM, GUC upgrade note, 2026-08-02). **"US fabless vendor" is Broadcom, and "bundled together at high margins" is the AVGO ASIC gross margin. The mechanism JPM describes is Google moving to Customer-Owned Tooling: three engagement models are live under the TPU v10 RFQ — (i) semi-CoT with [[MEDIATEK]] supplying SerDes/D2D/packaging/integration; (ii) compute-die back-end outsourced to pure design-service vendors (GUC, Alchip) on Turnkey-3; (iii) ⚠️ the full break-up, where "ONLY IP BEING SOURCED BY GOOGLE SEPARATELY… WITH FOUNDRY PROCUREMENT BEING HANDLED DIRECTLY BY GOOGLE, SIMILAR TO THE AMAZON TRAINIUM APPROACH."** ⚠️ **Model (iii) is the one that matters for this page: if the customer buys its own wafers from [[TSM]] and its own HBM, the ASIC vendor's revenue line collapses toward IP + services and the manufacturing/packaging margin accrues to the customer. Google ALREADY buys its HBM directly (confirmed on MediaTek's 07-31 call), and JPM notes the full-CoT opportunity "could be EVEN HIGHER" for non-TPU projects "given lower design complexity and IP dependence, AS ALREADY ILLUSTRATED FOR THE GOOGLE AXION CPUs" — i.e. the model has already been executed once at this customer, on CPUs.** **THE COMPETITIVE CLAIM ON v10 SPECIFICALLY: JPM says MediaTek's semi-CoT proposal "appears to offer an ATTRACTIVE TCO ADVANTAGE VERSUS BROADCOM, pointing to a high likelihood of securing the project and even potential share expansion," with the award expected in the coming months and mass production late 2029. Nomura the same day put GUC's chairlady on record as "NOT DENYING the company's opportunity in Google's TPU (which from our industry survey MIGHT BE TPU v.10)."** ⚠️ **How to hold this: it is a sell-side supply-chain read from analysts who cover the Asian challengers and therefore have a directional interest, the RFQ is unresolved, and Broadcom's own share of the current TPU generations is not disputed by any of it. But the argument is no longer "MediaTek is taking share at the margin" — it is "the buyer is re-architecting the contract so that no single vendor can hold that margin again." That is a different and more durable risk, and this page should carry it as the primary bear case on the ASIC franchise's terminal margin, cross-filed to [[GOOG]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]] and [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).** **One counterweight from the same house's own hierarchy: JPM expects "the highest value capture… in the next 3-4 years" to accrue to MediaTek with GUC/Alchip benefiting only "somewhat" — so the disaggregation is not yet a diffusion of the margin to everyone, it is a transfer of it to one named competitor.** Separately, corroborating a standing bear item on this page: **[[ANET]] checks say "supply has NOT improved, AVGO SILICON LEAD TIMES STILL >52 WEEKS"** (UBS, 08-01, already logged) — the franchise is supply-constrained at the same moment its largest ASIC customer is engineering around it. |
