  **Quarter synthesis:** flow turned incrementally more constructive through 06-27/06-29 (Jalapeño2/A16, memory-on-logic, Apollo 20GW, Jefferies BUY reiteration refuting the v9-delay/MediaTek-share bear case, Citi's #2-mega-cap memory-supply rank), then **reversed sharply on 07-01**: JPM (Gokul Hariharan) reports Broadcom's **Pumafish (TPU 9i) program cancelled or postponed**, directly contradicting the 06-29 Jefferies "on-track" framing and ceding 2028-29 v9-generation share to MediaTek (Broadcom retains a v10 role alongside MediaTek's Icefish); JPM/Hariharan also flags a possible v9-family SRAM ASIC ("Merope") going to **Marvell instead of Broadcom**. Both are concrete negative revisions to the custom-silicon share story, layering onto the pre-existing Redburn/HBM-in-BOM and SerDes-timing bear threads. Partially offsetting: **MS (Joe Moore, 06-30)** models **~14GW total 2028 compute** (~9GW TPU per Nowak) — a bull data point on absolute 2028 scale even as *share-mix* risk (Pumafish/Merope) rises — and **MS (Joseph Moore)** prefers AVGO on tightening inventory/supply alongside NVDA. Watch the **July 7 CEO/CFO NDR** (JPM desk) and Jefferies' teased "Revisiting the AVGO Bull Thesis" for management's response to the Pumafish reports. Mainstream press (Barron's 06-01/06-22) still corroborates the longer-run bull case (0.37 PEG, "scalpel vs Swiss Army knife" framing) but the marginal news of the day (07-01) is bearish on custom-silicon share, not demand. **Flow turned constructive again on 07-08:** JPM (Harlan Sur) reiterated Overweight after an investor meeting with Hock Tan / CFO Amie Thuener / IR Ji Yoo — ~50/50 XPU/GPU unit mix at the largest frontier labs next year, customers pulling in XPU tape-outs, Jalapeño validating ASIC execution (~9-mo tape-out) with the next OpenAI XPU taping out "very soon," Tomahawk 6 sold out, and ASIC margins held in-target — a broad rebuttal of the COT/share-loss/margin bear threads; JPM Sur also framed **TPU v9 as on track to ramp CY28** (400G SerDes working), which sits in direct tension with JPM Hariharan's 07-01 "Pumafish cancelled/postponed" report (unresolved intra-house). Separately the Apple–Broadcom 2031 deal was quantified at **$30B+** (Citi/Malik maintain Buy; @markgurman; DIGITIMES 07-09; Bernstein: "doesn't move the needle too much" but positive), with **$1.5B Colorado capex** and **15B+ US-made chips** and a custom-AI-silicon ramp seen ~2029/2030. **The 07-07 primary transcript** (Hock Tan / Amy Tanner, the call underlying Sur's 07-08 note) hardens the bull frame with first-party detail — only ~5 frontier developers = ~80% of demand, XPU units cross GPUs CY27 (~50/50 for the 5 buyers), COT "not a chance"/A8 "still in the Fab," Tomahawk 6 sold out, sole working 400G-copper SerDes, chip-margins-only into the $35B XPV, HBM not consigned. The offsetting bear mark of the week is **GS's 50/50 Broadcom/MediaTek split on the next-gen Google TPU** (07-09) — contested by US investors but another share-cede data point — while **MS (07-10) frames AVGO's AI business growing 80%+ next year and supply-constrained** (OW), a demand-scale positive. **07-11/07-13 add two constructive sector marks on demand (not share):** Mizuho reiterates Outperform (PT $530), with GOOGL's top-of-peer capex revision (+external TPU sales) and META's 14GW-2027E plan (~$200-300B, 40% above cons) both flagged **+ve AVGO**; and MS's *updated* Scale-Up Primer **4x's its own scale-up TAM to ~$70bn by 2030**, staying positive on AVGO into the 2027-28 ASIC ramps + networking pull-through, while endorsing AVGO's conservative copper-extension (200G→400G SerDes, 6m demo) view that pushes broad scale-up CPO to ~2028-29 — i.e. the demand/scale backdrop keeps improving even as the Pumafish/Merope/MediaTek share-mix debate stays open. **07-13/07-14 finally put a bull on the other side of that share debate:** Morgan Stanley (Joseph Moore) published **"In Defense of AVGO's ASIC Share" (reiterate OW, PT $502)** — MediaTek TPU participation is real but displacement fears are premature, AVGO keeps **~80% TPU share** on its HBM/packaging/scale moat (a close #2 to NVDA), and the YTD AVGO/NVDA underperformance is itself the TPU-share overhang unwinding; MS likens it to last year's over-drawn MRVL/Alchip Trainium debate. FinTwit corroborates (@BenBajarin: ">80%", @firstadopter quoting the note), and @dnystedt notes MediaTek's ASIC push is TSMC-wafer-gated — so the one-way "share-loss" narrative is now a two-sided debate. The offsetting bear mark is **@jukan05 (07-15)**: Samsung is weighing back-end design of the I/O die for Google's **2nm TPU v10 "Icefish," which Google is co-designing with MediaTek** (TSMC 1.4nm compute + Samsung 2nm I/O, MP ~2028) — so MediaTek's *next-gen* (v10) role keeps progressing even as MS defends near-term v7/v8 share. On networking, MS's scale-up-TAM raise gets independent corroboration (@QQ_Timmy: scale-up market >$70bn by 2030, 4x the ~$17bn-2029 est.) and AVGO's CPO/optical-engine positioning is reaffirmed by an ex-Intel expert (@AlphaSenseInc: AVGO + Intel strongest at CPO/NPO optical engines; NPO temporary, CPO the long-term path). **07-15→07-20:** MS (Joe Moore, "US memory selloff = entry point," 07-20) names AVGO with NVDA **"the best value in the market"** (compute over memory), valuing AVGO at **28x CY27e MW EPS $17.92 / ~26x non-GAAP $19.59** (a valuation frame, no PT change — MS OW $502 stands). Two read-throughs: Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (07-15) cites "Avago" as the spectacular-business comp memory should re-rate toward; and an ex-Intel VP expert call "EMIB vs CoWoS" (07-16) flags Google "diversifying beyond Broadcom" on TPU package design toward **Intel EMIB-T as a 2nd source** — a marginal packaging-diversification nuance on the standing Google-share-cede debate. **07-21/07-24 (SemiAnalysis, two notes — the networking leg of the thesis gets its hardest numbers yet):** the AMD Advancing-AI teardown (07-24) makes AVGO the **sole-source scale-up switch supplier to AMD's Helios rack** — "Broadcom is AMD's only choice" as the only merchant 100T switch with 200G SerDes — at **12 Tomahawk 6 102.4T ASICs across 6 trays per 72-GPU rack**, plus a **net-new retimer leg of >550 Broadcom Ethernet retimers/rack** because AMD's SerDes forces up to 85% of the backplane to be retimed, scaling to **288 switches on a 115.2T TH6 SKU across 576 GPUs/8 racks on MI500 (2027)**. Read together with the 06-25 BTG "no strong competition in switching" call and Hock's 07-07 "Tomahawk 6 sold out, no competitor at TH6," the merchant-networking leg is now the least-contested part of the AVGO story even as the Google-ASIC-share debate stays open — and note the socket sits at *AMD*, i.e. AVGO monetises merchant-GPU share gains it doesn't design. The Meta Infra note (07-21) cuts both ways and both legs are now on the page: negative on Rivos/COT bypass ambition (already logged, heavily hedged), but positive on sockets — Grand Teton "**increased their reliance on Broadcom**" (four PCIe switches/tray), and the DSF→NSF fabric shift moved Meta's spend **within** AVGO's portfolio (Jericho3-AI/Ramon3 → Tomahawk 5) rather than away from it, at the cost of admitting whiteboxes (Celestica) alongside Arista. Two backfill/structural markers added in the same pass, explicitly *not* current reads: **AWS's Sep-2025 decision not to use Broadcom for Trainium was made on price, not capability** ("Broadcom… has mopped up almost all of the major AI ASIC sockets"; Marvell/Alchip chosen as "somewhat weaker technology partners" offering "much more attractive pricing"), and **Google — unlike Amazon — runs its HBM through Broadcom's balance sheet**, the dated origin of the HBM-in-BOM share-cede mechanism Redburn identified in June (SemiAnalysis 2025-09-03); plus **AVGO's ASIC group alone spends an estimated $200-500M/yr on EDA tools/IP/emulation**, among the largest single EDA customers in the world (SemiAnalysis EDA primer, 2026-05-21) — a scale proxy for the design franchise. **08-03 (run-inbox, the Fubon PRIMARY call transcript) closes the window with the first hard-asset negative on AVGO itself, and with the bull case for a share recapture withdrawn by the same analyst who used to make it. The negative: Broadcom is "less bullish in expanding capacity" — specifically TEST capacity, with the planned 2H26 add "pushed out." That is narrower than the relay's blanket "less so for Broadcom" but harder, because test capacity is the last thing added before volume ships. The withdrawn bull case: Fubon's prior view that AVGO takes Google V10 share back from MediaTek rested only on Broadcom having 400G SerDes ready while MediaTek did not; MediaTek's 2H27 readiness (announced on its own call) removes the premise, and Fubon does not replace it. And when handed the bear question directly — could Google shift back to Broadcom, or in-house via Alchip/GUC, given 2028 broker ASIC estimates spanning $35-52bn — his answer names neither: the risk is Intel's EMIB production, because failure there sends MediaTek back to a TSMC that cannot supply 12-15mn TPUs, and the 2028 numbers come down for everyone. ⚠️ So the honest read of this call for AVGO is that its accelerator franchise is being priced off someone else's execution (Intel's, MediaTek's) rather than its own, while the parts of the ticker that clearly hold up are the switch/networking franchise (Tomahawk 6 is one of only three programs funding ASE's 50k-wpm expansion) and the V10 design itself, which Fubon has seen and MediaTek's he has not. One open disagreement left deliberately unresolved: Samsung 2nm dual-sourcing (07-28 KLAC call, yes) vs Fubon (still TSMC). **08-16/08-17 close the window on the two threads that have run through it all quarter, and neither moves a number.** On the ASIC franchise, **Mizuho reiterates Outperform $530 and names four programs ramping 2027E+ (TPU/MTIA/Jalepeno/Baltra)** while making AVGO one of only two named drivers of a **>75% y/y CoWoS 2027E** — supportive, additive, nothing superseded. On the financing thread the development is more interesting: **Epoch AI re-derived the AI XPV structure from public disclosure and landed on this page's exact numbers** ($6bn/$24bn/$4.5bn at T+1pp/5.75%/8.5%; $29bn max exposure), which **converts the $29bn from JPM's read of the 10-Q into a corroborated figure** and adds the three pieces the page lacked — **a draw schedule (~16 stages, ~$24bn out by summer 2027, so exposure peaks in mid-2027 rather than now)**, **a default waterfall that makes AVGO SECOND-loss behind used-rack residual value**, and **a 2.75pp upper bound on what the backstop is worth to lenders**. Net: the bull case (vendor-supported financing is what makes the TPU deployment fundable, and demand durability is not financing-constrained) and the bear case (a $29bn contingent exposure whose recovery assumption is un-modelled and whose peak is still ahead) are now built on the SAME disclosed structure rather than on competing guesses — which is the most useful thing that happened to this debate all quarter. **🔴 08-14/08-20 add the market infrastructure underneath that structure and one dated flag, and both belong in the synthesis rather than only in Risks.** **"Chip financing" via 4(a)(2) PRIVATE PLACEMENTS went from ZERO to $35BN year-on-year** (MS US Credit Strategy, 08-14) — inside an issuance table where US HY went 15→41 (+176%), leveraged loans 2→10 (+394%), non-USD IG hyperscalers 15→62 (+313%) and the total 189→382 (+102%). ➜ **That is what makes vendor-supported structures like the $30bn Anthropic/TPU backstop repeatable rather than exceptional: constructive for ASIC volume, and simultaneously a measure of how much contingent, off-public-market exposure the complex is taking on. MS's own thesis is that financing shifts "increasingly toward chips and components, with a larger role for private capital and high-quality credit support," with wider spreads "the natural release valve" and a stated preference for collateral-backed risk.** ✅ **The offsetting fact, and it should never be quoted apart from the $35bn: the [[NVDA]]+AVGO credit complex widened only ~20bp YTD versus +26/+29bp for the hyperscaler buckets and +1bp for broad IG — the market is differentiating inside tech and charging the chip VENDORS less than the buyers of their chips.** **🗓️ And MS's tracker names the print: *"For Single Name, ORCL and AVGO remain front and center ahead of their respective September [reporting dates]"* (08-20), into *"a busy fall and continued spread widening"* — so 09-02 is now a credit event as well as an equity one, which matters because the backstop draws in ~16 stages with ~$24bn expected paid out by summer 2027. Semiconductor issuance itself ran $42bn YTD (+57%).** **🔴 Separately, the competitive debate moved forward two generations: BofA's Asia call (08-20) reports a LIVE contest over whether [[AMD]], Broadcom, Alchip or GUC penetrates the [[GOOG]] TPU chain at V10 (~two years out), against MediaTek's claim to have been on V10's "triplet" architecture with Google since 2H last year and to own the integration.** ⚠️ **Two-sided and neither side is settled: Broadcom is still a named candidate two generations past the v9 loss (incumbency not conceded), but it is one of four contenders where it used to be the default and the dated head start is MediaTek's. The same call is the most directly bullish demand statement of the month for an ASIC franchise — hyperscaler capex still trending up with "no slowdown" despite the GOOG/META headlines, and spend rotating toward ASICs and toward monetisable inference because memory cost is rising while capex can no longer be funded from free cash flow. Held as a debate and a mechanism, not as a design win or a number: no AVGO rating, PT or estimate attaches to any of it.** ⚠️ **Guard-rail: MS's ~$1.4trn 2027 capex figure is an AGGREGATE across AMZN/GOOG/META/MSFT/ORCL/SPCX and NO per-name split is taken from that exhibit (column-interleaved chart) — it must not be fed into the FY27 AI-revenue debate as a customer-level number.**

  **Quarter synthesis — UPDATE 2026-09-01 (1 source folded in: FT · Ryan McMorrow, 2026-08-04 — ⚠️ journalism, no rating/PT/model; BACKDATED into the window, not new to the tape).** **No rating, PT or estimate on this page moves. One disclosed number changes meaning, and that is the whole entry.** 🔴 **This page has carried $128.1bn of purchase commitments ($55.2bn FY27 / $72.9bn FY28) since Wells Fargo's 08-18 preview as generic supply-chain evidence behind the ">$100bn FY27" line. The FT — dated two weeks EARLIER — attributes them: they relate "almost entirely" to Broadcom's APRIL agreement to buy 3.5GW of Google TPU hardware for Anthropic (4.5GW cumulative with the ~1GW already passed through Compute SPV in June at $35bn / ~1mn TPUs).** ➜ **That cuts both ways and both should be held. ✓ It is the strongest available corroboration of the FY27 bar: the supply commitment is contracted, filed and now customer-identified, and it independently supports Wells' inference that the "long-term contract (SINGULAR)" in the F2Q26 10-Q is Google. ⚠️ It also re-labels the line from diversified inventory to single-customer concentration — $128bn of Broadcom's own commitments standing behind one end-customer's lease payments — and it means the FY27/FY28 delivery split should be scored against the TPU GW/unit estimates on this page, not against total AI revenue.** 🆕 **On the contingent exposure the page already tracks, the FT adds a term and a shape rather than a new number: the guarantee is "RESIDUAL VALUE SUPPORT" on the two senior tranches, ~$30bn of the $35bn financing, and — the part that was missing — Broadcom's exposure DECLINES as Anthropic makes its lease payments. Set against Epoch's ~16-stage draw schedule (~$24bn out by summer 2027), the exposure profile is a HUMP peaking around mid-2027, not a plateau at the $29bn cap. Also net-new: Morgan Stanley arranged the vehicle, "an adaptation of the vendor-financing model Boeing and GE built" — the right mental model for a manufacturer lending credit rather than cash — and the hardware routes from Google THROUGH Broadcom, which is the mechanical reason the commitments sit on Broadcom's books at all.** ⚠️ **DISCIPLINE CARRIED FORWARD, unchanged: $128bn ÷ 3.5GW ≈ $36.6bn/GW is Broadcom's PURCHASE PRICE for complete Google rack systems and must never be compared with the $10–20bn/GW of AVGO content (Hock's range) or Jefferies' $11bn/GW — the same trap already flagged on Epoch's $34.5bn ÷ ">1 GW". And the FT's >$150bn of CHIPS and ~$200bn of CONTRACTS do not supersede Epoch's ~$50bn of DEBT; they measure different objects.** ➜ **Into the 09-02 print, the practical use is narrow and concrete: the purchase-commitment and RPO lines are now readable as a Google-TPU delivery schedule for Anthropic, so re-read the drawn balance and the commitment split each quarter rather than the headline cap.**

  🔴 **THE HEADLINE ITEM IS A VALIDATION, NOT A THREAT. SemiAnalysis was let into OpenAI's lab and benchmarked Jalapeño — the ASIC AVGO builds — on A0 silicon nine months after a November-2025 CoWoS tape-out, and it beat every NVIDIA, AMD and Google part they have tested on tokens per MW. For THIS page the load-bearing detail is not the benchmark, it is the BILL OF MATERIALS: the scale-up fabric is six 102.4T Tomahawk 6 ASICs per rack for the local domain plus two global switch trays, 128 ASICs per rack, a 2,048-XPU scale-up domain over copper + 1.6T optics + OCS. AVGO captures the compute die AND the switching. Production ramps through 2027, back-end loaded to Q4-2027; the next milestone is 100MW.** ⚠️ **The same article prices the argument honestly and both halves are recorded: on perf/TCO Jalapeño and Vera Rubin are head-to-head, and *"part of this TCO advantage comes from trading Nvidia's high margins for Broadcom's lower (though still high) margins"* — i.e. the ASIC wins partly by being cheaper, which is exactly the structural GM-dilution risk already on this page, restated by a bull.**
  🔴 **THE CREDIT BLOCK IS THE BIGGEST ANALYTICAL ADDITION, AND IT COMES FROM A DESK THIS PAGE HAD NOT YET NAMED. Morgan Stanley TMT CREDIT (Lindsay A Tyler) is the specialist that actually sized the contingent exposure: $19.3–24.2bn of maximum residual-value support PER GW ⇒ ~$290–480bn across the >20GW AI XPV platform (~$350bn headline on 06-19; "could surpass ~$300bn" on 08-05 with a second tranche reported). Treated as debt that is 3.3x gross adjusted leverage by FY28E against Moody's 2.5x threshold — though MS's own conclusion is that "the issue therefore would more be a hypothetical capital call, rather than the ratings impact."** ⚠️ **AND THE ARC IS THE POINT: the SAME desk recommended AVGO bonds on 05-29 ("a potential entry point"), closed every one of those recommendations on 06-19 once the platform was sized at >20GW, and by 08-05 called the level "not … a compelling re-entry point." Three weeks turned a buy into a close. That is a cleaner read on how the market re-priced the AI XPV disclosure than any equity note currently on this page.**

AVGO's backstop is second-loss against a residual value in a market that does not yet exist.**
